

===== PAGE: ability_delete_custom_transformations_now_available.html | Ability Delete Custom Transformations Now Available =====
## Ability Delete Custom Transformations Now Available
## What Is New?
 You can delete custom transformations that you create. The transformation wizard also includes new validation that prevents you from saving a transformation when the transformation name is blank.
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No change to the workflow process. The new deletion ability extends existing functionality.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 You can remove transformations that you no longer need.
## Where Do I See the Change?
 The Create Transformation screen now includes a Trash button. Clicking the button deletes the transformation.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are no additional relevant resources.
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: ability_to_delete_manually_entered_records_for_selected_domains.html | Ability to Delete Manually Entered Records for Selected Domains =====
## Ability to Delete Manually Entered Records for Selected Domains
## What Is New?
 For Trial Design domains such Trial Disease Assessments (TD) and Trial Arms (TA), and the Related Records (RELREC) domain, you can map data sets by manually mapping fields. This this update, you can delete these manually entered records individually or in bulk. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No change to the workflow process. The new deletion ability extends existing functionality.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 You can remove records that you no longer need.
## Where Do I See the Change?
 For the affected domains, when you click Map > Manually Enter Fields > Create and add records, each row now has a selection toggle. You can select one, multiple, or all records, and then click Delete Records(s) to delete the records from the data set.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are no additional relevant resources.
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: ability_to_delete_output_file_location_now_available.html | Ability to Delete Output File Location Now Available =====
## Ability to Delete Output File Location Now Available
## Description
 You can now delete output file locations you have access to. The locations list also updates the Output Locations column to instead show "Locations".
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the ability to configure output file locations. For details, see Exports Roles and Capabilities. 
## Advantages
 You can remove output locations that you no longer need.
## User Impact
 You can delete output locations from the Extractor's location list. When you review a location's actions menu, the Delete Location option appears. Otherwise your ability to create or manage output locations is unchanged.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Extractor Knowledge Space provides updated content to describe how to delete output locations: 
- Add SFTP Connection 
- Add S3 Connection 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: ability_to_export_transformed_data_sets.html | Ability to Export Transformed Data Sets =====
## Ability to Export Transformed Data Sets
## What Is New?
 You can publish configured data projects to save the configured and validated data sets therein. Once published, you can see your published data sets on the Study Projects screen > Data Source section > Curated Views list. You can download the data sets that the data project generates as a CSV extract.
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All users.
## Workflow Process
 No impact to the existing workflow process. You can publish data projects after successfully configuring transformations or derived variables.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 After publishing data project, you can e xtract the project's data sets as a CSV. You can use the extract in other applications such as Statistical Computing Environments (SCE) and visualization tools. You can also use the published data set as a data source for create more data sets. This saves you time in configuring complex transformations by making the process more modular . 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 A Publish button appears for each given data project on the Study Project screen. Clicking More shows other options including Subscribe and Export as CSV.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 The Platform Workbench Publication provides new content to support this feature:
- Export Dataset Snapshots from Project 
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: ability_to_pause_and_resume_schedule_jobs.html | Ability to Pause and Resume Schedule Jobs =====
## Ability to Pause and Resume Schedule Jobs
## Description
 You can now pause scheduled and repeating jobs. You can also resume paused jobs. You can pause jobs from the Export Jobs screen or from a specific job's Job Configuration Screen.
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the ability to manage export jobs. For details, see Exports Roles and Capabilities.
## Advantages
 You can pause jobs that you no longer need, and restart jobs when you need them again. These additional options give you more control regarding when the Medidata platform extracts your data and do not require intervention by Medidata Support.
## User Impact
 Existing scheduled jobs are unchanged. You can pause and resume jobs from the Export Jobs screen or a specific job's Job Configuration screen. Otherwise your ability to create or manage export jobs remains the same.  
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Extractor Knowledge Space provides updated content to describe how to pause and resume jobs: 
- Review Exports List 
- Review Export Configuration 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: about_data_connect.html | About Data Connect =====
## About Data Connect
 Data Connect is a part of Clinical Data Studio, and unifies data discovery, ingestion, transformation, and exports. It provides interoperability across various data sources and systems. Users can explore internal and external datasets, import non-Medidata data sources, and transform datasets into industry or custom data models. Users can then activate  non-Medidata datasets for downstream Medidata experiences, or export and share datasets to external systems. 
## What Is Data Connect?
 Data Connect is a suite of software capabilities that combine intuitive data syncing capabilities with powerful data transformation and export tools enabling users to:
- Ingest any data quickly and easily. 
- Transform data with minimal effort. 
- Extract data from various sources. 
## What Is the Purpose of Data Connect?
 Data Connect's capabilities provide cross-functional operational teams tools to: 
- Manage and import data from external sources. 
- Alleviate bottlenecks by reducing the burden on technical resources to prepare collected clinical data for later submission 
- Reduce iterations and handoffs between user groups through real-time data previews and validation. 
- Provide end-to-end visibility throughout the data life cycle. 
- Reduce overhead to orchestrate clinical data assets for downstream analysis. 
- Download data from the Medidata platform in a central location. 
## Who Uses Data Connect?
 Data Managers, clinical data scientists, biostatisticians, data scientists, programmers, and more use Data Connect. 
## How Does Data Connect Work?
 Data Connect consists of a singular workspace that allows you to seamlessly work across the following modules: 
- Datasets – R eview existing datasets that you access to including from Rave, external data sources, and transformed data sets. 
- Imports – M anage and import data from external sources. 
- Exports – Create and manage export jobs to extract your data from the Medidata platform. 
- Transformations – C reate data sets, and transform them depending on your needs: 
- Derived Datasets – E xplore existing data sets and create data sets based on a combination of various parameters. 
- Unit Conversion – Configure unit conversations to transform data set fields from one unit into another unit for later mapping. 
 Important :  Unsupported Data 
 Data Connect does not support variables such as coding dictionaries, coded terms, and code lists. While you can import datasets that includes these data, you cannot see or use them in Data Connect. However, these data can appear as-is in RBQM and Data Surveillance when you activate the associated dataset. For details, see Unsupported Variables in Data Connect . 
## What Are the Benefits of Data Connect?
 Data Connect:
- Provides access to data from Medidata data sources, such as Rave, and simplifies importing data from any non-Medidata source, such as labs or another Rave system. You can configure data transfer agreements into Data Connect to validate ingested data automatically. 
- Enables clinical data managers build complex listings across multiple datasets without the need for programming. Clinical programmers and biostatisticians use Data Connect's no-/low-code data transformation
capabilities to create custom datasets. Data Connect maintains complete traceability of all data transformations so users can always see what and how data was transformed. 
- Allows users to create export jobs to enable the extraction of data sets from the Medidata platform  for any studies and environments they have access to . 
## How Do I Get Started?
 See one of the following: 
- Grant Access to Data Connect 
- Datasets 
- Imports 
- Exports 
- Transformations

===== PAGE: access_data_connect.html | Access Data Connect =====
## Access Data Connect
 Note : You require permissions to access and perform specific actions in each Data Connect capability. To learn more, see  Data Connect Roles and Capabilities .
 To access Data Connect: 
- Do one of the following:
- If you are not yet logged into Medidata:
- Log in to iMedidata.com 
- Click the Data Connect tab in the tab bar below the Medidata Home page welcome message. 
- In the Clients table, click the client name containing the study you want to access. 
- In the Study Groups table, click the study group name containing the study you want to access. 
- In the Studies table, click the study you want to access.
 The Data Connect > Datasets page shows by default. 
- If you are currently working in another Medidata app:
- Click the Data Connect tab in the tab bar below the study banner.
The Data Connect > Datasets page shows by default.

===== PAGE: activate_datasets.html | Activate Datasets =====
## Activate Datasets
 You must activate datasets so that you can use them for other capabilities compatible with Data Connect, such as Data Surveillance and RBQM . You can activate the following dataset types: 
- Imported third-party EDC data 
- Transformed datasets 
## Activation Considerations
## Dataset Upgrade Considerations
- Derived datasets created before May 23, 2025 must be upgraded and then reactivated to enable direct Rave querying in Data Surveillance. 
- Derived datasets created on or after May 23, 2025 do not need to be upgraded. 
- Derived datasets using the following operations from Rave datasets will be enabled for direct Rave querying in Data Surveillance:
- Join 
- Union 
- Replicate or Filter 
- Medidata recommends upgrading no more than five datasets at a time per study. To upgrade more datasets, wait for any queued upgrades to finish. 
## Other Considerations
- Any records associated with identified unregistered values are unavailable for the downstream applications that you activated the dataset for. 
- Data Connect only activates the latest dataset version. When there is a new dataset version after activation, a notification prompting you to update the activated dataset appears. 
- An activated dataset can have six possible states: Activated, Upgrade Available, Inactive, Error, and Blanks. For details on each, see Dataset Statuses . 
- If you or another user applies a sequence number configuration onto a dataset, the resulting dataset records can be initially inconsistent with the expected record count since the system actively processes the dataset. The system eventually completes processing, and records and record count become consistent. 
- The activated datasets have a limit of 100 columns for downstream applications. 
## Activate Dataset
 Important : Activation for Medidata DQM  
- The  Medidata system considers records with  an empty visit_name as unregistered and thus they do not appear in Data Surveillance or RBQM. Data Connect considers records with an empty visit_name as unregistered. 
- Mixing fields that contain raw dates with those that contain interpolated dates result in query submission failures in Data Surveillance and RBQM. For more, see  Interpolated Variables Behavior in Data Surveillance and RBQM 
- If you do not see all visit date values after activation, check your dataset mapping. For details, see  Missing VISIT_DATE Values . 
- Access Data Connect , and  click   Datasets   in the left-hand menu. 
- Select a dataset.
 You can filter the dataset list by clicking one of the following categories:
- All – Show all datasets. 
- Alerts  – Show all dataset that has errors or warnings. 
- Internal – Show datasets from internal sources from within the Medidata platform, such as Rave data. 
- SDTM / Enriched View / Custom Data Model / Derived Dataset – Show datasets from the respective data transformation type as produced through Data Connect's Transformations capability. 
- External – Show datasets from sources imported through Data Connect, such as Labs data. 
- Sensor – Show datasets from the Sensor Cloud. 
- Select the  Activation tab. 
- Click the More icon, and select  Activate to open the Dataset Activation  screen. 
- Map source fields as necessary, and click  Validate to run validation testing.
 Note : Field Mapping to Clinical Data Studio Metadata 
 The following table highlights how fields map to downstream data in Clinical Data Studio, such as RBQM and Data Surveillance. 
 Field 
 Metadata in Clinical Data Studio 
 | site_id 
 | Site
 | subject_id 
 | Patient
 | visit_name 
 | Performed Event Name
 | visit_date 
 | Performed Event Date
 | record_sequence 
 | Item Group Report Key
 Ensure that you map visit_date if you plan to use the dataset for visualizations in Clinical Data Studio. 
- Review the data preview tabs as necessary.
 Tip :  Addressing Unscheduled Visits 
 You can add unscheduled visits during the activation process, but Data Connect does not have a visit scheduler. Instead, you can do one of the following:
- Create a derived dataset, and sort by dates. 
- While activating the dataset, configure the dataset sequence by sorting by dates.  
- Address any issues in the Quality Control tab by transforming the target data: 
- Identify a row with a target field to transform. 
- Click the row's More icon, and click Transformation Instruction to open the Edit Transformation screen. 
- Apply transformation instructions as necessary. 
- Close the Edit Transformation screen and return to the Dataset Activation Screen. 
- Click  Activate .

===== PAGE: add_data_source.html | Add Data Source =====
## Add Data Source
 To import data from external sources, you must first establish an SFTP or S3 data source connection.
 Important :
- Currently Imports supports only SFTP, FTPS and, S3 connections. 
- A data source connection can have multiple incoming datasets.  
## Access the Data Source Configuration Wizard
- Access Data Connect , and click   Imports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click   Add data source . 
## Add Data Source
 SFTP FTPS S3 
 SFTP
- On the add data source screen, enter data source name, and click Continue . 
- Click SFTP to import files from an SFTP connection, and enter below details: 
- Host – The connection's host URL 
- Port – The connection's port number 
- Folder path – The connection's folder path 
- Username – The connection's username 
- Password –The connection's password 
- Click Test Connection .
 Tip : Recheck the connection details if the authentication fails. You can continue configuring your data source even without testing the connection.  
- After the test is successful, click Add data source .   
 FTPS
- On the add data source screen, enter data source name, and click Continue . 
- Click FTPS to import files from an FTPS connection, and enter below details: 
- Host – The connection's host URL 
- Port – The connection's port number 
- Folder path – The connection's folder path 
- Encryption mode – Select the encryption mode as needed.  
- Username – The connection's username 
- Password –The connection's password 
- Click Test Connection .
 Tip : Recheck the connection details if the authentication fails. You can continue configuring your data source even without testing the connection.  
- After the test is successful, click Add data source .   
 S3
- On the add data source screen, enter data source name, and click Continue . 
- Click S3 to import files from an S3 connection, and enter below details: 
- Access key – AWS access key ID 
- Secret access key – AWS secret access key 
- Region – AWS region 
- Bucket name – S3 bucket name 
- Folder path – S3 folder path 
- Click Test Connection .
 Tip : Recheck the connection details if the authentication fails. You can continue configuring your data source even without testing the connection.  
- After the test is successful, click Add data source .   
## Next Step
 Add Dataset

===== PAGE: add_dataset.html | Add Dataset =====
## Add Dataset
 After you configure your data source connections, you land on the Connection screen. From there, you can add expected datasets from a data source connection. 
## Add Dataset
 Important :
- Imports support CSV and SAS ( .SAS7BDAT and .XPT ) files. 
- Ensure that the original source file is available in source folders when ingesting. 
- If the selected file is large, you can preview only the first 1000 rows, but all data imports after successful configuration. 
- Click Add dataset . 
- On the add dataset screen, enter the Dataset name. 
- Click Select file , and select the file from the SFTP or S3 locations to configure.  
- Click Select file. 
## Configure Dataset Specification
 On the Configure Dataset Specification screen, you can digitize the data transfer agreement available for this dataset . You can configure the expected data type for each column of the imported dataset, and select the columns that constitute a unique record as dataset keys. 
- On the left-hand menu, click  Auto-detect. The system assigns a data type for each column.
 Tip : Verify the data types assigned to the columns manually. To modify the expected data type for a column:
- Click the Validations column and select the expected data type. 
 Data Type 
 Definition 
 | Codelist 
 | 
 Select this data type to define a set of acceptable values for a column.
 | Date/DateTime 
 | 
- Select this data type for columns with dates. 
- Select a date time format for the column from the list. The date format must match the format of the imported column. 
 | Float 
 | Select this data type for columns with any decimal values. 
 | Integer 
 | Select this data type for columns with integer values. 
 | String 
 | Select this data type for columns with alphanumeric values. 
- Select the unique columns to be the dataset key for each import.
 Important : Changing key fields after you publish datasets and import records can cause errors in RBQM and Data Surveillance.
 Tip : The system validates the input automatically. Select Filter invalid rows  to view all the rows with errors.
 Note :
- If the selected file is large, you can preview only the first 1000 rows for the dataset key. I f the rows beyond the first 1000 rows contain duplicate keys in the dataset key column ,  then the system returns errors after you import the dataset. 
- You can continue configuring your dataset even with the errors. 
- Click Continue . 
## Define Importing Criteria
 You can set up search criteria, to import datasets recurrently from a data source on the importing criteria screen. 
 Important : The imported file must match the configuration defined for the dataset. 
- Select Includes or Excludes. 
- Enter the search key word for the file. 
- To add more importing criteria, click + . 
- Repeat steps 1 and 2. 
- Click Add dataset > Start import recurrence .
 Tip : To import the datasets later, select Start later .

===== PAGE: add_datasets.html | Add Datasets =====
## Add Datasets
 As necessary, you can add datasets from the datasets list. You can add imported datasets and derived datasets .
## Add an Imported Dataset
 You can add datasets from external sources.
- From the All Datasets screen, click Add dataset >  Import to go to the Imports screen where you can add external data sources. For details, see  Import External Data with Imports . 
## Add a Derived Dataset
 You can add datasets that contain transformations at both the dataset level and field level, such as with aggregated multiple other datasets or changed data structures. 
- From the All Datasets screen, click Add dataset >  Derived datasets to go to the data transformation canvas. For details, see  Generate Derived Datasets .

===== PAGE: add_ftps_connection.html | Add FTPS Connection =====
## Add FTPS Connection
 You can configure exports to send a copy of extracted files to a server using FTPS . To do so, you must first add one or more FTPS connections to your client division. After adding one or more FTPS connections, the list of the connections is available to other users within your study group.  
## Add Connection
 Important : 
- You are responsible for the access policies, file retention policies, and security of your external connection clients. Medidata does not manage your clients' configurations. 
- To ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers:
- Update your clients' permit-list and deny-list to accept dynamic IP addresses to ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers. 
- The external connection's account must have full access to the parent directory specified in the connection's settings folder path and all subfolders. This includes read, write, update, and delete privileges.
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Connections to enter the Connections screen. 
- Click Add Connection , and enter the following settings:
- 
- 
 Connection Name 
 This name appears to a user when creating an export. Ensure that this name is meaningful to the other users.
- 
- 
 Data Transfer Direction 
 Configures the connection to allow data export, data import, or both.
- Export Data  –  Select to create a connection for exporting data. Only applicable for exports. 
- Import Data –  Select to create a connection for importing data. Only applicable for Ingestor import. 
- 
 Connection Type 
 Select   FTPS. 
- 
 Host 
 The destination server's full host name.
- 
 Port 
 The standard port is 22. 
- 
 Folder Path 
 The relative path from the connection's root to the destination folder for the connection.
 Important : The path can be case-sensitive depending on your configuration.
- 
 Encryption Mode 
 Encrypts the connection for data import, export, or both.
- Implicit – The information exchange between the user and the server is encrypted. 
 Note : Use this encryption mode for Medidata hosted FTPS server.
- Explicit  – The user must send a request to switch to a secure FTPS connection to share sensitive information. 
- 
- 
 User Name 
 The username with write access to this target folder. Use connection-specific user names for these write-access connections to the server.
- 
 Password or Private Key 
 Depending on your external connection policy specify either the password or a private key for the connection's user. This is a plain text field, with no validation.
- Click Add Connection . 
 Tip : More Actions for Connections 
 After creating a connection, you can perform the following actions:
- Test Connection – You can test connections on the Connections screen:
- Find the Connection you created, and click Options > Test Connection . 
- Delete Connection – You delete connections on the Connections screen:
- Find the connection you created, and click Options > Delete Connection . 
## Next Step
 Configure Export to Use SFTP Connection

===== PAGE: add_partial_dates_for_published_derived_datasets.html | Add Partial Dates for Published Derived Datasets =====
## Add Partial Dates for Published Derived Datasets
 You can now add raw dates for the published derived datasets activated for DQM.
 Important : This configuration update is only necessary for derived datasets published before the Data Connect 2025.1.0 release. For newer projects, you ensure that you select raw and interpolated dates as applicable when creating new derived datasets for DQM.
- Access Data Connect and click Transformations on the left-hand menu. 
- Identify a project to add the raw date field.  
- Click the More icon > View / Modify > Yes . 
- To modify the configuration, select the first transformation node. 
- Search for the field with interpolated dates. 
- Edit the alias name by adding "_Int" as a suffix and click Apply . 
- Search for the field with raw dates. 
- Edit the alias name by adding "_Raw" as a suffix and click Apply. 
- Repeat steps 3 to 8 where for other transformation nodes as necessary. 
 Tip : A pply the configuration after each update, making bulk updates can result in errors or unintended output. 
 Important :
- Make sure the alias for interpolated dates has "_INT" suffix and a matching field name with a raw date, such as  LBDAT and LBDAT_INT. Data Surveillance and RBQM capabilities in DQM relies on the exact field name pattern for the algorithm to function. 
- Do not remove or add nodes to the current derived datasets if you require the update to reflect in the original derived dataset in DQM. 
- Transformations do not support "yy" datetime formats. 
 Note : The added fields cascade to the next transformation node.   
- Select the final transformation node and search for the field and ensure that both interpolated and raw fields are available.
 Tip : If you have previously created derived variables that use the previous interpolated dates, reset the derived variable configuration to make sure the validation passes before you publish.
- Go to the Transformation landing page. 
- Identify the project that you modified. 
- Publish Projects .
 Note : After publishing, wait for around 10 minutes to view the new version of the dataset with new configuration in datasets module. 
- Activate the derived dataset to see the changes in Data Surveillance and RBQM.

===== PAGE: add_s3_connection.html | Add S3 Connection =====
## Add S3 Connection
 With the S3 connection configured for use on the Medidata system, you can add the connection to the external file connection list.
## Add Connection
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Connections to enter the Connections screen. 
- Click Add Connection , and enter the following settings: 
- Connection Name  – Enter a name to appear on the Connections screen. 
- Data Transfer Direction – Select Export Data . 
- Connection Type – Select S3 (Export Data only). 
- AWS Account # – Enter your AWS account number. 
- Bucket Name – Enter the S3 bucket name. 
- Folder Path – Enter the path within the S3 bucket to deliver data to.
 Note : Enter the Bucket Name and Folder Path as configured in your AWS console as they are  case-sensitive for S3 connections. 
- Click Add Connection .  
 Tip : Additional Actions for Connections 
 After creating a connection, you can perform the following actions:
- Delete Connection  –  You delete a connection on the Connections screen: 
- Find the connection you created, and click Options > Delete Connection . 
## Next Step
 Configure Export to Use S3 Connection

===== PAGE: add_sftp_connection.html | Add SFTP Connection =====
## Add SFTP Connection
 You can configure exports to send a copy of extracted files to a server using a secure file transfer protocol ( SFTP ). To do so, you must first add one or more SFTP connections to your client division. After adding one or more SFTP connections, the list of connections is available to any other users within your study group.  
## Add Connection
 Important : 
- You are responsible for the access policies, file retention policies, and security of your external connection clients. Medidata does not manage your clients' configurations. 
- To ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers:
- Update your clients' permit-list and deny-list to accept dynamic IP addresses to ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers. 
- The SFTP account must have full access to the parent directory specified in the connection's settings folder path and all subfolders. This includes read, write, update, and delete privileges.
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Connections to enter the Connections screen. 
- Click Add Connection , and enter the following settings: 
- 
 Connection Name 
 This name appears to a user when creating an export. Ensure that this name is meaningful to the other users.
- 
- 
 Data Transfer Direction 
 Configures the location to allow data export, data import, or both.
- Export Data  –  Select to create a connection for exporting data. Only applicable for exports. 
- Import Data –  Select to create a connection for importing data. Only applicable for Ingestor imports. 
- 
 Connection Type 
 Select   SFTP. 
- 
 Host 
 The destination server's full host name.
- 
 Port 
 The standard port is 22. 
- 
 Folder Path 
 The relative path from the connection's root to the destination folder for the connection.
 Important : The path can be case-sensitive depending on your configuration.
- 
 User Name 
 The username with write access to this target folder. Use connection-specific user names for these write access connections to the external server.
- 
 Password or Private Key 
 Depending on your external connection policy specify either the password or a private key for the connection's user. This is a plain text field, with no validation.
- Click Add Connection . 
 Tip : More Actions for Connections 
 After creating a connection, you can perform the following actions:
- Test Connection – You can test connections on the Connections screen:
- Find the connection you created, and click Options > Test Connection . 
- Delete Connections – You delete output connection on the Connections screen:
- Find the connection you created, and click Options > Delete Connection . 
## Next Step
 Configure Export to Use SFTP Connection

===== PAGE: amazon_s3_extraction_location_option_now_available.html | Amazon S3 Extraction Location Option Now Available =====
## Amazon S3 Extraction Location Option Now Available
## Description
 When configuring export jobs, you can choose to deliver files to an Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) location that you own. 
## Affected Roles
 All platform role with the Extractor Admin building block.
## Advantages
 You have more options to deliver your data extracts, especially if you use Amazon Web Services to host your data.
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, you can select the Extractor Admin building block, which grants permission to select S3 locations as an export target.
 In Extractor,
- In the Settings tab, you can select and configure the S3 location type when creating output locations. 
- In the New Export Job workflow > Job Name step > Export Location section, you can choose to have the job delivery data to an S3 location. 
 Note :
- When configuring S3 locations, you must add a location and then enter the location settings. You cannot edit existing SFTP locations to convert them into S3 location configurations. 
-  You can only set S3 locations as a data delivery target for export jobs. S3 locations are incompatible with import jobs. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Extractor Knowledge Space provides new and updated content to support this new data delivery location: 
- Updated
- Name Outputs 
- New
- Configuring Exports to Deliver to Amazon S3 Connections 
- Prepare Amazon S3 Bucket 
- Add S3 Connection 
- Configure Exports to Use S3 Connection 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: archive_export.html | Archive Export =====
## Archive Export
 You can deactivate exports you no longer need.  Archived scheduled exports no longer run. You cannot manually run an archived export.   
 Important : You cannot reactivate archived exports.
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu to land on the Exports screen.
 Note : You can only see the exports you created unless you have Super User permission that allows you to see all exports in the study group. For details, see Data Connect Roles and Capabilities . 
- Identify an export to deactivate, and click the Options icon >  Archive export . 
- The export no longer runs, and appears on the Export screen's Archived tab.
 Tip : You can also archive an export from its Configuration screen.

===== PAGE: audit_data_extraction_now_available.html | Audit Data Extraction Now Available =====
## Audit Data Extraction Now Available
## Description
 You can configure export jobs to extract audit data from your studies. You can extract Study Level Master Audit data and CTMS Audit data. Study Level Master Audit data includes team membership and site address data. CTMS Audit data includes service provider, visit, and document audit data. 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Extractor.
## Advantages
 You can extract Audit data from your studies.
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, you can add the following building blocks to your customer-defined platform role:
- Extract Cloud Admin Audit Data – You can extract Audit Master data. 
- Extract CTMS Audit Data – You can extract Audit CTMS data. 
 In MEDS Extractor, y ou can select the Audit Master Data and Audit CTMS data types while creating export jobs. 
 Audit Master data extracts reflect data entered into Cloud Administration such as client division, study site, and role information in three files: 
- Study Environment Sites 
- Study Environment Site Addresses 
- Team Memberships 
 Audit CTMS data extracts reflect data entered with CTMS applications such as Study Management and Site Monitoring, and include service provider, site visit, and document information in three files: 
- CTMS Service Providers 
- CTMS Site Visits 
- CTMS Documents 
 When testing this feature, consider comparing the extracted data from this export job type to data entered into the respective applications. 
## Special Considerations
 When you select the Audit Master Data type, you cannot select any other data type. Similarly, when you select the Audit CTMS data type, you cannot select any other data type.

===== PAGE: client_division_level_extraction_now_available.html | Client Division Level Extraction Now Available =====
## Client Division Level Extraction Now Available
## Description
 You can configure export jobs to extract data from the client division level. Exporting data from the client division level extracts data from all studies within the client division. Client division level extractions are only available for the Master and Audit data types.
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 With client division level extractions , you can extract and download data from every study of your client division without needing to select each study. 
## User Impact
 You can only set export jobs to extract from client division level during the job creation process. You cannot edit existing export jobs to extract from the client division level. This feature does not affect existing export jobs.
 During the New Export Job workflow's Select Source step:
- You can now choose between Study Level and Client Division Level extractions.
- When you select the Client Division Level option, the Study, Environment, and Add Study Environment options disappear. To change client divisions, you must use the client division menu from the application's top toolbar.
 The Export Jobs page includes both study level jobs and  client division level jobs. The new Export Level column appears between the Job Name and Data Type columns, and denotes if the job extracts at the study level or at the client division. You can also filter the jobs list by Export Level. 
 The Job Configuration page includes the following changes:
- For all export jobs, the Job Configuration page includes the Export Level field. The Export Level field denotes if the job extracts at the study level or at the client division level. 
- For client division level export jobs, the Client Division field replaces the Study or Environment fields. The Client Division field shows which client division the export job applies to. In addition, the Job Configuration page does not group data sets by study environment. 
 The Job Run History page > View Details panel includes the following changes: 
- For all export jobs, the Job Run History page includes the Export Level field. The Export Level field denotes if the job extracts at the study level or at the client division level. 
- For client division level export jobs, the Client Division field replaces the Study or Environment fields. The Client Division field shows which client division the export job applies to. In addition, the Job Run History page's details panel does not group data sets by study environment. 
 When exporting Client Division Level Master Data, the extracted files have the following different data objects: 
- Client Division Scheme Organizations instead of Study Organizations. 
- Client Division Scheme People and Addresses instead of Study People and Addresses 
- Client Division Scheme Users and Addresses instead of Study Users and Addresses. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The following articles have been created or updated in the MEDS Extractor Knowledge Space to support this feature:
- New
- Create Export Job at the Client Division Level  
- Select Source for Client Division Level Jobs 
- Select Data for Client Division Level Jobs

- Set Export Type for Client Division Level Job  
- Name Outputs for Client Division Level Job  
- Extract Client Division Master Data - Quick Start

- Updated
- Review Exports List 
- Review Export Configuration 
- Review Export Run History 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

 | Text 
 | 50 
 | The first name of the user who updated the record. 
 | Last Name 
 | Text 
 | 50 
 | The last name of the user who updated the record. 
 | Updated By App 
 | Text 
 | 256 
 | The name of the app that performed the update. 
 | Date (UTC) 
 | Timestamp 
 | 23 
 | The date and time when the audit was created, for example: 16-APR-2021 17:41 UTC.
 The system stores the date and time in UTC, and shows both in your local device's timezone.

===== PAGE: configure_export_job_to_use_s3_connection.html | Configure Exports to Use S3 Connection =====
## Configure Exports to Use S3 Connection
 Once the S3 connection is on the connection list, you can use the connection when configuring exports.
## Configure Exports
 While creating an export, on the Name Outputs step: 
- Enable the Export Connection  toggle. 
- Select Deliver to S3 Connection . 
- Select a Connection from the menu. 
- Optional . In the Path field, enter an extension to the connection's path. 
 When the system delivers files, the files appear in the path extension as shown in the Full Path preview.
 Note : Enter the Path as configured in your AWS console as it is case-sensitive for S3 connections. 
- Enter the remaining export details, and click  Create Export .   
 Tip : For greater detail on the Name Outputs step, see  Name Outputs .

===== PAGE: configure_export_to_use_ftps_connection.html | Configure Export to Use FTPS Connection =====
## Configure Export to Use FTPS Connection
 Once the FTPS connection is on connections list, you can use the connection when configuring exports.
## Configure Export
 While creating an export, on the Name Outputs step: 
- Enable the Export Connection  toggle. 
- Select Deliver to FTPS Connection . 
- Select a Connection from the menu. 
- Optional . In the Path field, enter an extension to the connection’s path.  
 When the system delivers files, the files appear in the path extension as shown in the Full Path preview. 
- Enter the remaining export details, and click Create Export .   
 Tip : For greater detail on the Name Outputs step, see  Name Outputs .

===== PAGE: configure_exports_to_use_sftp_connection.html | Configure Exports to Use SFTP Connection =====
## Configure Exports to Use SFTP Connection
 Once the SFTP connection is on the connections list, you can use the connection when configuring exports.
## Configure Exports
 While creating an export, on the Name Outputs step: 
- Enable the Export Connection  toggle. 
- Select Deliver to SFTP Connection . 
- Select a connection from the menu. 
- Optional . In the Path field, enter an extension to the connection’s path.  
 When the system delivers files, the files appear in the path extension as shown in the Full Path preview. 
- Enter the remaining export details, and click Create Export .

===== PAGE: configuring_exports_to_deliver_to_amazon_s3_connections.html | Configuring Exports to Deliver to Amazon S3 Connections =====
## Configuring Exports to Deliver to Amazon S3 Connections
 When configuring exports, you can choose to deliver files to an Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) connection that you own. To successfully add an S3 connection, you must prepare the S3 connection on your AWS instance, and work with Medidata to connect it to the Medidata platform. From there, you can add the connection to the external file connection list.

===== PAGE: configuring_exports_to_deliver_to_ftps_connections.html | Configuring Exports to Deliver to FTPS Connections =====
## Configuring Exports to Deliver to FTPS Connections
 When creating exports, you can configure the export to send a copy of extracted files to a server using a file transfer protocol (FTPS). After adding one or more FTPS connections, other users within your study group can use them when configuring exports.
 Important : To configure the list of FTPS connections, your platform role must include the Extractor Admin building block. For details, see  Grant Access to Data Connect and Data Connect Roles and Capabilities .
 Tip : Manage FTPS Connections with API Resources 
 You can also manage SFTP connections from the Medidata platform using Clinical Cloud API resources. For details, see the Create External File Location for Data Import and Export Jobs workflow and the Export File Locations resource on the Clinical Cloud API Knowledge Space.

===== PAGE: configuring_exports_to_deliver_to_sftp.html | Configuring Exports to Deliver to SFTP =====
## Configuring Exports to Deliver to SFTP
 When creating exports, you can configure the exports to send a copy of extracted files to a server using a secure file transfer protocol (STFP). Medidata's implementation of SFTP is FTP over a Secure Shell Protocol, also known as SSH. After adding one or more SFTP connection, list of connections is available to other users in your study group.
 Important : To configure the list of SFTP connections, your platform role must include the Extractor Admin building block. For details, see  Grant Access to Data Connect and Data Connect Roles and Capabilities .
 Tip : Manage SFTP Connections with API Resources 
 You can also manage SFTP connections from the Medidata platform by using Clinical Cloud API resources. For details, see the Create External File Location for Data Import and Export Jobs workflow and the Export File Locations resource on the Clinical Cloud API Knowledge Space.

===== PAGE: configuring_external_file_connections.html | Configuring External File Connections =====
## Configuring External File Connections
 When configuring exports, you can choose to deliver files to an external file connection that you own. After configuring the external connection outside of the Medidata platform, you can add the connection to the external file connection list. You can then use that connection when configuring exports. 
- Configuring Exports to Deliver to Amazon S3 Connections 
- Configuring Exports to Deliver to FTPS Connections 
- Configuring Exports to Deliver to SFTP 
 Tip :  Troubleshooting Common Connection Issues 
 A common issue that occurs is misconfigured delivery location servers for external connections. Ensure that the delivery location is properly configured before adding it as a location. 
- SFTP / FTPS – FTP servers must be accessible outside your organization's private network. Medidata cannot whitelist IP addresses or VPN tunnels to an external server. 
-   S3 – S3 connections require other configurations on your side. You must complete the client-side AWS configuration before adding the connection. For details, see Prepare Amazon S3 Bucket .

===== PAGE: create_data_extracts_in_meds_extractor.html | Create Data Extracts in MEDS Extractor =====
## Create Data Extracts in MEDS Extractor
 Note : This feature appeared as part of MEDS Extractor 2019.1.0.
## Description
 MEDS Extractor enables the creation of data extracts. You can set extracts for specific data sets from the studies and environments. These extracts run immediately.
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Creating data set extracts provides a pipeline to pull your data from Medidata's data repository, replacing otherwise manual integrations. 
## User Impact
- In Cloud Administration, you can:
- Add the Export Data permission to your customer-defined platform role which grants access to MEDS Extractor; and 
- Select MEDS Extractor from the Actions dropdown menu from within your assigned study environment. 
- In MEDS Extractor, you can:
- Create exports; and 
- Export data sets to CSV files for download to your local drives. 
## Special Considerations
 You must have an unblinded role assigned to you in at least one study to access MEDS Extractor.

===== PAGE: create_export.html | Create Export =====
## Create Export
 You can create exports at the client level or the study context level.  By creating an export at the client level, you can extract data from every study within the client. By creating an export at the study level, you can extract data from a subset of studies within the client. 
 After you create the export, and the export successfully runs, you can then download extracted datasets. 
## Create Export
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Create Export . 
- Complete the Create Export wizard.
 Tip : For a walkthrough on completing the wizard, see  Select Data Source  and follow the workflow.
## Export Creation Workflow
## Available Data Types
 The data you can export differs when you export at the client level or the study level. For details about the data objects that appear in the exported data files, see Available Exportable Data Objects .
## Next Step
 Download Data Extracts

===== PAGE: create_export_jobs_with_externalized_api_endpoints.html | Create Export Jobs with Externalized API Endpoints =====
## Create Export Jobs with Externalized API Endpoints
## Description
 You can use more Medidata's Clinical Cloud API endpoints to create export jobs and extract data using Extractor resources. The Medidata platform processes and delivers files identified through the endpoint. You also create, update, and test SFTP locations. 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with permission to use Clinical Cloud RESTful API Resources. For details, see  Get Started with Clinical Cloud RESTful API Resources .
## Advantages
 You can programmatically export files using Medidata's API, giving you more options to download data from the Medidata platform.
## User Impact
 The addition of externalized Extractor resources does not affect your use of the Extractor user interface. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None. You can find details on the  Clinical Cloud API Changelog .
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: create_job_ui_enhancements.html | Create Job UI Enhancements =====
## Create Job UI Enhancements
## Description
 Enhancements to user interface (UI) when using the extract job wizard include the following updates: 
- Improved variable drag-and-drop functionality in the New Export Job wizard's Name Outputs step. 
- Renamed the Data Type "Rave EDC [Raw]" to "EDC [Raw]" as data can originate from EDC systems other than Rave. 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the Manage Extracts permission.
## Advantages
 The usage of variables in the Name Outputs step works consistently, and the dashed border of the drag region disappears when all variables are used in output names.
## User Impact
 See the Description section.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: create_new_version_for_a_configured_dataset.html | Create New Version for a Configured Dataset =====
## Create New Version for a Configured Dataset
 You can create a new version for the updated datasets when the imported datasets show errors due to changes between imported dataset and the configured dataset. 
 Important :
- You cannot edit published datasets. 
- Imports automatically de-activates streaming of an older dataset to Data Surveillance when you create a new version. 
- Before creating a new version of a dataset, that dataset is not in use by Data Surveillance. If necessary, deactivate the dataset before creating a new version.  
- Access Data Connect , and click   Imports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click the More icon of the data source, and select View. 
- Click the More icon of the dataset, and select Edit and test specifications . 
- On the Dataset screen, click the More icon > Edit. 
- On the Edit Dataset Schema pop-up, click Proceed to editing . 
- On the Configure Dataset Specification screen, configure the dataset specification for the new file.  
- Click Publish .
 Important :  Changing key fields after you publish datasets and import records can cause errors in RBQM and Data Surveillance. 
 Tip : To view the data from the previous versions of the dataset.
- Access Data Connect , and click   Imports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click the More icon of the data source, and select View. 
- Click the > arrow to the left of the dataset name. 
- Click the version you want to view.

===== PAGE: custom_variable_fields.html | Custom Variable Fields =====
## Custom Variable Fields
 The article lists the fields you can enter when adding a custom variable.
 Field 
 Description 
 Required 
 | Alias 
 | The variable's short form name. 
 | Yes 
 | Description 
 | The variable's description to clarify the alias as necessary. 
 | Yes 
 | Variable Data Type 
 | The variable's data type: 
- Numeric – A data type that represents a number, with multiple formats you can select in the Variable Format field. 
- String – A data type that represents text data type. 
- Boolean – A data type that represents logical true and false. 
- Date – A data type that represents the date. 
 | Yes 
 | Variable Format 
 | 
 The variable's format; the selections depend on the data type. 
- Integer – A numeric format denotes numbers without decimals. 
- Float – A numeric format that denotes numbers with decimals. 
- String – A textual data format denotes a sequence of characters, digits, or symbols; and which a system always treats as text. 
- Boolean – A logical data format that denotes one of two values: TRUE or FALSE. 
- Date – One of several date format variations that contains or omits the time. Key date parts include: year, month, day; and for time: hour, minute, seconds, and milliseconds. For supported formats, see  Date Time Variable Formats Supported . 
 | Yes 
 | Target Variable Requirement 
 | The variable's mapping requirement:
- Required – You must map the target variable before publishing the dataset. 
- Permissible – You can map the target variable for data collection purposes, although it is not necessary for downstream applications. 
- Expected – You intend that target variable to include future values. 
 | Yes 
 | Reason 
 | A description for why you created the variable. 
 | No 
 | Unique Identifier 
 | Denotes if the variable serves as a unique identifier within the dataset. 
 | No

===== PAGE: custom_view_creation_now_available.html | Custom View Creation Now Available =====
## Custom View Creation Now Available
## What Is New?
 You can use Platform Workbench to generate data sets based on your own data models by performing source-to-target data mapping and transformations for domains within a data model you configure. You can create these custom data sets from scratch or based on a template model. 
 After creating a data project, you can perform tasks such as: 
- Add custom variables with configurable data types, formats, and requirements. 
- Configure a custom target variables by mapping from source data fields, current target domain variables, and entered values. 
- Customize transformations using no-code modules for field transformation. 
 When you refresh the preview while building data mappings, Platform Workbench performs quality checks on your edits based on your configurations. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 There is no change to the workflow process. This feature extends existing project creation capabilities.  
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 You can create any data model on the Medidata platform that can match your downstream pipelines as necessary. Moreover, you are no longer locked to creating mappings based on the SDTM data model, which is not ideal for analysis use cases.  
## Where Do I See the Change?
 When you create a data project in the Transformations, you can select the Custom View project type. When selecting this project type, you can either create the data model from scratch or use another custom data model created within your client division.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 Supplementary help articles will be available at a future date.
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: data_connect-transformed_data.html | Data Connect =====
## Data Connect
 You can export data objects containing data from datasets transformed by Data Connect's Transformation capability. 
 Important :
- Because of the user-driven nature of transformed data, there is no way to list possible output from this data object. The data that you receive from extracted transformed data object depends on the configuration of the source data project. For details about transforming datasets, see Transformations . 
- The Data Connect data type can be exported in CSV, SAS XPT and dataset JSON formats.

===== PAGE: data_connect.html | Data Connect =====
## Data Connect
 Data Connect is a part of Clinical Data Studio, and unifies data discovery, ingestion, transformation, and exports. It provides interoperability across various data sources and systems. Users can explore internal and external datasets, import non-Medidata data sources, and transform datasets into industry or custom data models. Users can then activate  non-Medidata datasets for down stream Medidata experiences, or export and share datasets to external systems.

===== PAGE: data_connect_-_centralized_error_handling_of_datasets.html | Data Connect - Centralized Error Handling of Datasets =====
## Data Connect - Centralized Error Handling of Datasets
## What Is New?
 Users can view end to end status of datasets from Data Connect's Imports, Exports, and Transformations directly in the Datasets module.
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No impact to workflow processes.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 As a single dataset can be used in multiple Data Connect modules, it is time consuming to check each module separately for errors. This feature enables centralized error handling across Data Connect with clear error messages.
## Where Do I See the Change?
 To access this feature, access Data Connect and click Datasets on the left-hand menu.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 Use the following links to find new and updated content in support of this Feature:
- Review Datasets 
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: data_connect__help_menu_launches_contextually_relevant_knowledge_hub_user_assistance_directly_in_app.html | Data Connect: Help Menu Launches Contextually Relevant Knowledge Hub User Assistance Directly in App =====
## Data Connect: Help Menu Launches Contextually Relevant Knowledge Hub User Assistance Directly in App
## What Is New?
 On all Data Connect pages, The Help menu > Help On This Page option launches directly within the Data Connect user interface the contextually relevant Knowledge Hub user assistance related to the page that you are currently working in.
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All Data Connect users.
## Workflow Process
 On any Data Connect page, when you click Help > Help On This Page, the How Can We Help You? pane appears in the bottom-right corner of the page. The pane directly opens the contextually relevant Knowledge Hub page or provides a list of two or more relevant pages that you can choose to view and interact with. You can:
- Click the Expand icon to expand the user assistance page view to full screen.
- When viewing in full screen, you can click the Expand icon to condense the Knowledge Hub content back to the pane view. 
- Click the PDF icon to download the Knowledge Hub content shown in the How Can We Help You Pane? as a PDF. 
- Perform a keyword search to find the content you need in the Knowledge Hub. 
- Click the New Tab icon to open the Knowledge Hub content in a separate browser tab. 
## Other Applications
 No other applications are impacted.
## How Do I Benefit?
 The Help On This Page option launches contextually relevant Knowledge Hub user assistance directly within the Data Connect user interface so that you:
- Never have to leave the Data Connect user interface to find the user assistance you need. 
- Quickly and efficiently self-serve and find the product knowledge you need to optimally perform your work. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 On any Data Connect page, when you click Help > Help On This Page, the How Can We Help You? pane appears in the bottom-right corner of the page. The pane directly opens the contextually relevant Knowledge Hub page or provides a list of two or more relevant pages that you can choose to view and interact with.
 Tip : Click image to expand.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are no additional relevant resources.
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: data_connect_released_features_index.html | Data Connect Released Features Index =====
## Data Connect Released Features Index
 This article lists the features and enhancements of Data Connect as an exportable table with the following highlights:
- Feature Article – The article link. Click to go to the article's details. 
- Product – The product's name. 
- Version – The product version the update pertains to. 
- Change Type – Feature, enhancement, or technical change. 
- Functionality or Module – The specific functionality area or category the update pertains to. 
- Summary of Changes – A summary of what changes the feature, enhancement, or technical changes introduced. 
 Tip : You can cross-reference the product version with the releases history to determine when a particular capability was released. 
 Important : Features and enhancements introduced before Data Connect 2026.1.0 do not use the current tabular format and therefore do not appear on this table. For a list of all feature and enhancements, see Data Connect Released Features . 
 * 
- Add filter 
- Reset all filters 
- Disable all filters 
- Reset column sorting 
- Modify Settings 
- Copy the filter URL 
- Export to PDF 
- Export to CSV 
- Export to Word 
- Print 
- Documentation 
- What's new 
- Rate our app 
- Enable all filters 
- Export to PDF 
- Export to CSV 
- Export to Word 
- Print 
- Documentation 
- What's new 
- Rate our app

 Product 
 Version 
 Change Type 
 Functionality or Module 
 Summary of Changes 
 | Imports: Enhanced Veeva API Requests 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.9
 | Enhancement (Updated Functionality) 
 | Imports
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter :
 Enhanced Veeva API requests to consistently use the same study name identifier for testing connections, retrieving dataset lists, and running the study data extract job. 
 Note : This is a technical change with no impact to existing connections.
 | Transformations: Upstream Editing for Filter, Union, and Join Operators 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.2.1
 | Enhancement (Updated Functionality) 
 | Transformations
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter :
 Data Connect enables users to edit Filter, Union, and Join operators when positioned upstream in the transformation canvas lineage.
- Filter Operator : Edit filter criteria directly on upstream nodes (for example, changing criteria from Value > 5 to Value > 10 . 
- Union Operator : Add new incoming data set paths to an upstream union node. 
- Join Operator : Modify join keys by swapping or adding keys on upstream join nodes. 
 | Imports: Performance and Reliability Enhancements, and Preparation for Future Capabilities 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.6
 | Enhancement (Updated Functionality) 
 | Imports
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter :
 Internal and technical changes to improve product performance, reliability, accuracy, and stability.
 | Introducing the Enhanced Release Notes Experience 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.1.0
 | N/A (Education Content)
 | None (Education Content)
 | 
 The release notes include several formatting updates to address reported challenges and issues with the Medidata Release Notes experience. The changes appear across multiple pages for the release notes.
- Tabular Structure – Pages that describe changes, such as this article, now appear in a tabular structure with renamed or added sections.  
- New sections 
- Product – The product the change applies to. 
- Version – The code product version that the change applies to. 
- Change Type – The type of change: 
- Feature 
- Enhancement 
- Technical change 
- Functionality or Module – The area of the product that this section affects, such as a particular module within the product or a subsection within the module. 
- Impact to Existing Data or Settings – Any changes that an update introduces to existing data or settings that need review. 
- User Impact Assessment – A high-level summary of the change's impact. 
- Changed sections 
- "What's New?" is now "Summary of Changes" 
- "How Do I Benefit?" is now "Benefit to Users" 
- "What's the Impact?" is now "Summary of User Impact" 
- "Users/Roles" is now "Impact to Users and Roles" 
- "Workflow Process" is now "Impact to Workflow Processes" 
- "Other Applications" is now "Impact to Other Applications" 
- Where Do I See This Change? is now "Impact on Interface" and appears in the Summary of User Impact section 
- New Released Features Indices – Release notes now include new Released Features Index articles — one for a specific release, and another for the entire product across releases. These articles list all changes relative to the page's scope as a downloadable table. 
- Feature Release Notes Parent Page – For Feature Release Notes, the home page includes new links to other reference articles such as the released features indices and webinar details, where applicable. 
 | Transformations: Edit Derived Variables in Intermediary Nodes 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.4
 | Enhancement (Updated Functionality) 
 | Transformations.
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 You can now edit derived variables in intermediary nodes when the variables are not consumed downstream. 
 | Imports: Interface and Technical Enhancements for Data Connect 2026.3.3 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.3
 | 
 Enhancement (Updated Functionality)
 | 
 Imports
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter :
- System and Performance Upgrades : U pgraded background task engine, starting with Veeva connector data syncs to ensure more resilient, predictable data processing and faster issue resolution. 
- Activity Audit Logging : Added system-wide audit tracking for key user actions and data changes.  
- Accurate Status Feedback : Files containing only headers or nonvalid patient records now show a Warning status rather than misleading success or error messages. Uploading an empty file now shows a helpful warning message rather than triggering an error. 
 | Imports: Veeva Direct Connect Enhancements for 2026.3.0 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.0
 | Enhancement (Update Functionality)
 | Imports 
 | 
 Data Connect improves Veeva Direct Connect and the dataset list view: 
- Datasets imported from Veeva EDC now use the Form OID exclusively (Form Name in Veeva) in data connect imports and clinical data ingestion flow. 
- The dataset list view displays the Form OID as a badge and the descriptive form name next to it for readability. 
- The system provides an improved error message when imports fail because of missing codelist columns. 
 | Transformations: Updates to Unit Conversion Workflow, Veeva EDC Datasets, and Other Enhancements 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.0
 | Enhancement (Update Functionality) 
 | Transformations 
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 | Unit Conversions 
 | You can create, edit, delete, and publish unit conversions within derived dataset projects using a redesigned interface. 
 | Veeva EDC datasets 
 | 
- Support now shows Form OID in data preview screen for Veeva datasets. 
- Updated data source panel now shows Form OID instead of form name for Veeva datasets. 
 | General 
 | 
- The Data preview screen now shows a  last updated timestamp indicator. 
- Improved performance for derived dataset projects through backend optimizations. 
 | Developer Center: New Python Library and Other Enhancements 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.3.0
 | 
 | Developer Center 
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 Data Connect introduces the Python SDK and updates the R SDK.  Both software development kits (SDKs) are available as beta releases in Developer Center . 
 The Python SDK library: 
- Provides access to Data Connect data. 
- Lets you register data products created in Python back in Data Connect through the Library.  
- Is now compatible with a native IDE for running the transformation script. 
 The R SDK library now:
- Presents a data frame containing invalid records from dry publish and publish operations. 
- Updates the Dry Publish and Publish responses to include valid record counts and duplicate record counts. 
- Creates a new version of a Custom Code dataset if you modify any key fields after the initial Publish operation. 
 The Documentation page now includes new Python library documentation and updated R library documentation. 
 | Imports: Technical Changes and Preparatory Work for Future Capabilities in Data Connect 2026.2.5 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.2.5
 | Technical Change
 | None.
 | 
 Data Connect routinely includes internal and technical changes to improve performance and optimize back-end processes. Data Connect 2026.2.5 introduces the following internal code changes:
- Migration of the Imports application infrastructure to a dedicated AWS account. 
 | Transformations: Internal and Technical Changes in Data Connect 2026.2.6 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.2.6
 | Technical Change
 | None.
 | 
 Data Connect routinely includes internal and technical changes to improve performance and optimize back-end processes. Data Connect 2026.2.6 introduces the following internal code changes:
- Updated SQL logic for improved math operation handling.  
 | Exports: Interface and Technical Enhancements for Data Connect 2026.2.1 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.2.1
 | 
 Enhancement (Updated Functionality)
 | Exports
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter :
- Exports Interface : Exports shows Veeva Connect datasets consistently with Data Connect Datasets, Imports, and Transformations. 
- Exports Wizard : You can schedule hourly exports to run at 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, and 12-hour intervals. 
- Exports : 
- Data Connect migrated the backend database for Master Data, Rave CTMS, and EDC (Raw) data types 
- Data Connect improves handling for Date columns. 
- The Resume/Pause Export button on the Job Details page now works as expected. 
 | Transformations: Derived Variable and Field Count Enhancements 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.2.0
 | Enhancement (Update Functionality)
 | Transformations
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 | Derived variables 
 | 
- From the Canvas after creating a dataset, you can now select  Add Derived Variable from the Operator list. 
- Helper fields: 
- Calculated Fields are now called Helper Fields. They are available on the same screen as the Derived Variable. This variable does not appear in the output dataset/node. You can use it with multiple derived variables. 
- Logic Condition: 
- Using multiple data types no longer requires Helper fields for data type conversion. Data Connect auto detects data types when you enter a Value.  
- The Concatenate option now has both Simple and Advanced options. 
- The Substring menu selection has By Position and By Delimiter options. 
- You can enter expressions while using the Math calculation. 
- Rank operations have new names: 
- RANK is now GROUP [First/Last].
- GROUP is now GROUP STATS [Min/Max/Avg/Sum]. 
- New Keys is now Grouping fields. 
- Select fields is now Select output fields. 
 | Field count 
 | 
- The Data Preview screen includes a field count beyond the count of records. 
 | Imports: Compatibility Enhancements and Preparation for Future Capabilities 
 | Data Connect
 | 2026.1.5
 | Enhancement (Update Functionality)
 | Imports
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 Support for Alternative Country Names : Imports now support alternative country names. Alternate country spellings automatically map to the appropriate ISO code. 
 In addition, this release provides the infrastructure updates to support the following future capability: 
- Updates to Support Bulk Query Veeva Direct Connect : You can now generate bulk queries within the Data Surveillance and RBQM modules of Veeva EDC for data imported with Veeva Direct Connect. You can also retrieve and use the full history of available Veeva EDC queries.
.
