===== PAGE: generate_custom_code_projects.html | Generate Custom Code Projects =====
## Generate Custom Code Projects
 Important : This capability is available as a limited release for selected users.
 Data Connect's Custom Code data project serves as the 
 destination and container 
 for datasets you externally create and transform with Data Connect R Software Development Kit (SDK) . This article covers the different aspects and purposes of the Custom Code project type. 
## Overview
 This project hosts all datasets published into Data Connect by the available SDKs through your development environment using the dry_publish() and publish() functions from the SDK library. For details, see the respective Data Connect Library reference file in Data Connect.  
 Before creating the Custom Code project, you must first generate an authentication token from  Developer Center and use it to initialize the connection to the Data Connect R or Python library. After  making the connection, you can use your development environment to perform transformations wing the R library. When you are ready to publish the R-transformed dataset back to Data Connect, create a Custom Code project in Transformations. 
 After creating the Custom Code project, you can generate a Project  token , which you use in your development environment as the project_token parameter when calling the dry_publish and publish() function . For details, see the R Data Connect Library reference file in Data Connect > Developer Center > Documentation. 
 The project view shows the output dataset published from R into Data Connect and the source datasets that you used to generate that output. You can only see the source datasets that Data Connect supports, such as Rave datasets, derived datasets ,  third-party imported data , and custom code datasets .
## Publish Dataset
 You must use the Custom Code project to publish datasets transformed with your development environment into Data Connect.
- If not done already,  assign   the Manage Custom Code Projects building block to access and enable the   Custom Code project   in Transformations. 
- In your development environment, perform any necessary operations and validations on the dataset according to R Data Connect library reference. 
- In Data Connect > Transformations, create a Custom Code project in Transformations. 
- View the project, and copy the project token in the Access Keys panel. 
- In your development environment, paste the project token as the project_token parameter for the dry_publish() and publish() functions, and run the respective functions. 
 Important : 
- Token Generation : When you create a Custom Code project, the system automatically generates the project token. This token is specific to the study and to the project. 
- Publishing Requirements :
- Only the user that creates the project can publish datasets into it. 
- The published dataset must meet validation rules, including having a unique dataset name and passing all necessary key fields. 
## Other Considerations
 After successfully publishing the dataset, Data Connect categorizes it with the Custom Code dataset type, from which you can perform functions such as activation. The below highlights considerations pertaining to these functions and downstream impact, such as with Data Surveillance and RBQM.
- Versioning – You can only have one custom code dataset per project. Successfully publishing an R-transformed dataset creates version 1 of the dataset in Data Connect. If you make a change to the dataset such that both the dataset name and dataset metadata is unchanged, then Data Connect appends the dataset without creating another version of the dataset. However, if you do change the dataset such that the dataset name remains the same and metadata changes, then Data Connect creates another version of the same dataset. 
- Activation for Data Surveillance – If the published dataset includes compatible key fields such as patient, sites, and visits, then you can activate the dataset for Data Surveillance. 
- Module Usage and Restrictions for Data Surveillance and RBQM –
- You cannot submit queries on datasets transformed by custom code. 
- You cannot use these datasets when using the following modules in Data Surveillance and RBQM: Derived Variables, Stake Datasets, and Event Analysis Mapping. However, you can use these datasets for Listings, KRIs, QTLs, Critical Data, and self-service visualization.

===== PAGE: generate_derived_datasets.html | Generate Derived Datasets =====
## Generate Derived Datasets
 You can create derived datasets based on a combination of various parameters and using capabilities including: 
- No-code dataset operations, configurations, and transformations 
- The ability to add more variables to the derived dataset 
 With derived datasets, you can combine and transform datasets to support downstream use cases and analytics.
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
 Important : 
- Creating a derived dataset does not affect the source datasets. You cannot directly change the source dataset with Data Connect. This is to maintain the datasets' data lineage and to uphold the principle of traceability. 
- Data Connect does not support the Time datatype. Data Connect converts any Rave datasets with Time datatype to String datatype. 
## Transform Datasets 
- Expand a Derived Dataset project, and click View / Modify . 
- From the Data Source panel, search or use the folders to find forms to add to the Target panel.  
- Use the Drag icon to drag datasets onto the Target panel.
 Tip : Clicking the View icon shows the form’s data source details. 
- Click the More icon for one of the forms in the Target panel, and select an action: 
- Join 
- Union 
- Group - Min, Max, Avg, Sum 
- Group - First/Last 
- Replicate or Filter 
- Pivot 
- Unpivot 
- Use the action wizard to perform operations on the selected form.
 Note : 
 Considerations 
- Operations such as Union require that you include more than one dataset on the Target panel. 
- The Union and Unpivot operations support a maximum of five datasets per operation. 
- When performing the Unpivot operation, Medidata recommends against unpivoting many columns in a single project. If you must unpivot multiple columns, nest unpivots by:
- Creating a project to unpivot a subset of columns; 
- Creating another project unpivoting other columns against the result of the original unpivot operation. 
- Repeat as necessary. 
- When performing the Pivot operation, Medidata recommends that the pivot variable contains no duplicates, including values that differ only by letter case. To prevent this, apply a field transformation to convert all values to either lowercase or uppercase before pivoting. 
 Limitations 
- You cannot perform Join or Union operations on two identical forms. You can, however, perform Join or Union using two identical forms after applying any of the following operations to one of the forms: Replicate, Filter, or Pivot. 
- If you have a dataset with multiple rows per patient, such as Adverse Events, you cannot turn the dataset into a single row per patient with concatenation. 
- Close the action wizard. 
- Repeat steps 2 to 6 for more operations as necessary. 
## Add and Map Variables
- Click a target view, and click List View .
 Important : You can only click List View on datasets that have no children: that is, datasets that are results from previous operations. For example, if you perform a Union between Dataset 1 and Dataset 2 to create Dataset 3, you can only see the List View for Dataset 3. 
 In addition, if you performed further transformations on Dataset 3 to result in Dataset 4, then you can only see the List View for Dataset 4 Dataset 3 is now a parent.
- As necessary, click the More icon > Add Variable , and add custom variables to the dataset: 
- From the target variable list screen, click the More i con, and select Add Variable . 
- Enter the custom variable details . 
- Click Add Variable . 
- Add more variables as necessary. 
- Map one or more values to each variable in the Mapped Fields and Values column. 
- As necessary, transform the custom variable , and resolve any quality issues. 
- Click Update target view mapping to save your settings. 
## Next Step
 Publish Project

===== PAGE: grant_access_to_data_connect.html | Grant Access to Data Connect =====
## Grant Access to Data Connect
 You must to give users relevant building blocks to access, use, and manage Clinical Data Studio and its capabilities.  To access and use Clinical Data Studio, users' customer-defined platform role  must include the appropriate building blocks.  For a detailed list of applicable Clinical Data Studio building blocks, see:
- Data Surveillance Building Blocks and Permissions 
- RBQM Building Blocks and Permissions 
- Data Connect Building Blocks and Permissions 
## Configure Roles
 You must configure customer-defined platform roles before you can assign the roles to users. You can either create roles or update roles. For more information on configuring roles in Cloud Administration, see Assign User Roles. 
## Create Role 
 You can create new roles specifically to access Extractor. 
- Log into  iMedidata.com . 
- Click your study group in the  Study Groups  pane. 
- Click  Manage Roles . 
- Select  Add Roles >  Add New Role . 
- Complete the  Add a Role  form:
- Enter a role Name. 
- Select any Standard Role.
- Select one or more building blocks. 
- Click  Save . 
## Assign Roles
 You must assign customer-defined platform roles to users for the users to access Extractor. You can assign users are either the client division level or the study level.
 Important : To extract data with the Medidata Rave and Audit CTMS data types for a given study, you must also use Cloud Administration's Manage Access tools to set Site and Country Access to all sites and countries.
## Assign Roles at Client Division Level
 For more information on assigning roles at the client division level in Cloud Administration, see  Assign User Access to Study Groups .
- Navigate to a client division in Cloud Administration. 
- Click Manage People   in the left-hand navigation. 
- Open the  Add Person menu  and click   Add Access . 
- Select the invitation email locale.
 Note :  Information about Email Invitation Locale 
 The invitation menu only sets the locale of invitation emails for users without existing iMedidata accounts. Existing users receive notifications in their current locale.
- Enter user email addresses into the Email field.
You can enter up to 25 email addresses. 
- Select  Client Division . 
- Select any applicable roles from the Roles menu. 
- Click Assign Access . 
## Assign Roles at Study Level
 For more information on assigning roles in Cloud Administration, see  Assign User Access to Study Environments and Sites .
 Tip : You can also upload a user data file to Cloud Administration to update multiple users. For details, see  Upload Users to a Study .
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Click your study in the  Studies  pane. 
- Click  Manage Access . 
- Click  Add Users >  Add Access 
- Type or paste the emails of the users you want to add into the  Emails field.
 Tip : You can enter up to 25 email addresses at once.
- Select the environment you grant access. 
- Select the applicable roles from the  Roles  dropdown. 
- Click Assign Access .

===== PAGE: hourly_extractions_now_available.html | Hourly Extractions Now Available =====
## Hourly Extractions Now Available
## Description
 When creating export jobs that contain CTMS or Audit data, you can set the job to repeat every hour.
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Extractor.
## Advantages
 With the hourly repeat cycle, you can systematically extract CTMS or Audit data multiple times throughout the day.
## User Impact
 During the New Export Job workflow's Schedule Job Run(s) step, you can select the Hourly frequency.  The Hourly frequency is only available for the Audit data types or any of the CTMS data types. If your export jobs includes any other data type, the Hourly frequency becomes unavailable. 
## Special Considerations
 MEDS Extractor data extracts are only available for 24 hours after a successful job run. However, you can direct MEDS Extractor  to save a copy of the   extracted data to your selected SFTP destination. Extract copies saved to SFTP are   not subject to this 24-hour download time limit. Client SFTP administrators specify the file retention periods and access permissions for SFTP locations. The setup of, and access to, SFTP repositories is not under Medidata control. For details on setting up SFTP, see Configure SFTP Locations for Extracts, and the Name Outputs step of the   Create Export Job workflow.

===== PAGE: import_external_data_with_imports.html | Import External Data with Imports =====
## Import External Data with Imports
 You can automatically ingest external datasets from a data source connection. 
 Important :
- Your platform role must include Manage Data Sources building blocks to access the Imports tab and ingest external data. For details about granting access see Grant Access to Data Connect . 
## File Requirements and Compatibility
 You must prepare your external data to be compatible with the listed formats, sources, and import types.
 Category 
 Details 
 | Supported File Formats 
 | 
 Clinical Datasets 
- UTF-8-encoded Comma-separated value (UTF-8 .CSV) 
- SAS (.sas7bdat or sas.xpt) 
 Note : The SAS7BDAT file format is a SAS proprietary file format. You can import this format using Imports, but expect formatting inconsistency around the date, datetime, and numeric data types. To ensure maximum compatibility, use the XPT or CSV format. Medidata recommends that you use the XPOT function in SAS to export the XPT file format.
 Metadata Datasets 
- UTF-8-encoded Comma-separated value (UTF-8 .CSV) 
 | Supported Sources 
 | 
- SFTP 
- S3 
- FTPS 
- Veeva EDC 
 | Supported Import Types 
 | 
- Cumulative – Each import cycle imports all available records, even unchanged records. 
 | Maximum Supported File Size 
 | 
- 1 GB for CSV 
- 600 MB for SAS7bdat and XPT 
 | Notes 
 | For CSV files, the system supports special characters as long as the file uses structural quoting. That is, you can use commas ('), backslashes (/), double quotes ("), and so on if you enclose the field in quotes.
## Import Data Workflow
 To set up an import, you must add a data source and add datasets in the data source. There must be at least one dataset file in the data source during configuration. The file must be a CSV and can be a sample or test file.
## Import Process
 After adding and enabling a dataset, the system attempts to load a file from the data source once each hour. You can also manually load the file from the data source's status screen. The system performs a cumulative refresh – that is, it expects a full refresh of the file from the source for every import – identifying which files and records are new, updated, or removed. 
 Note : The system considers a file new when: 
- It meets the import criteria as defined during dataset configuration; and, 
- Its Last Modified date in the source folder is greater than the last time the data source was checked. 
 If you change a dataset's specifications or import criteria, the system does not automatically attempt to reload the last processed file. You must wait for the next hourly refresh cycle or manually trigger the refresh for the system to pick up the changes.
 To manually trigger the refresh cycle:
- Go to Data Connect > Imports, and select a dataset. 
- From the dataset's More menu, click  Trigger data source synchronization .
 You can only manually refresh non-Veeva EDC data sources.
 Loading times can vary because factors can affect loading times such as: 
- The number of new records versus deleted records 
- The number of columns 
- The number of non-string data types. 
 If loading does not complete within six hours, please contact Medidata Support. 
## Third-Party EDC Import
 For details, see Import Third-Party EDC Data and  Import with Veeva EDC Direct Connect 
## Dataset Activation for Data Surveillance
 When you have at least one successfully imported dataset, you can activate the dataset for use by Data Surveillance. For details, see  Activate Datasets .

===== PAGE: import_third-party_edc_data.html | Import Third-Party EDC Data =====
## Import Third-Party EDC Data
 You can automatically ingest third-party EDC data for any Non-Medidata study with SFTP, FTPS and, S3 locations. You can configure visits, sites, and patients metadata with Data Connect's Imports experience.
 Tip :  If you use Veeva EDC, you can use Data Connect’s Veeva Direct Connect capability. For details, see  Import with Veeva EDC Direct Connect . 
## Before You Start
## Unsupported Clinical Data Studio Capabilities
 Unsupported Capability
 Workaround
 | Medidata does not support system KRIs ingested from third-party EDC systems:  
- AE Entry Delay 
- Visit Entry Delay 
- Missing Visits 
- Out-of-window Visits 
- System and Non-System Query Rate 
- Non-System Query Delay 
- Number of SAEs per Month 
- Number of AEs per Month 
 | None 
## General Limitations
- While you can import multiple form types, Data Surveillance only supports forms with patients, sites, and visits. 
 Important : Data Connect does not support multiple sites per patient. You must tag a single site as the primary site in the source system to ensure patients reflect the latest site. If you do not update the source system, then patients associated to the most current site appear as unregistered values in Datasets. 
## Special Considerations
- When you update the dataset in your third-party EDC system, you must reactivate the dataset in Data Connect to propagate the changes in your downstream applications. For details, see Activate Datasets . 
- Data Connect automatically tags third-party EDC datasets as a Derived Dataset when it requires extensive transformations before being available for activation. 
- Data Connect shows a banner when your Rave studies have not been onboarded for compatibility with third-party EDC datasets. 
- All third-party EDC studies must be in their own client – that is, completely separate from the Medidata platform. This is because the system assumes the source of the metadata is Data Connect. For example, if you import sites from Data Connect and use Cloud Administration to update client division organizations (CDOs) and sites, then data inconsistencies can occur. 
## Prepare Metadata Datasets
 Before you can import third-party clinical data, you must import the following three metadata datasets:  
- Visits – Adds all planned visits within the study. 
- Sites – Adds all sites expected for the study. 
- Patients – Adds all patients and their associated primary site. 
 You can only configure each metadata dataset once. If necessary, you can edit the mapping after creation to resolve errors. After creating the data source, you can configure and import clinical datasets for an EDC source as necessary.  
 You do not need to import metadata datasets and clinical datasets in a specific order. However, you cannot activate clinical datasets for Data Surveillance until you also successfully import the associated metadata.  
## Mapping Requirements for Each Metadata Dataset
 Visits 
 The data source file from the third-party EDC must include a list of planned visits that can map to the below target fields. The Medidata system does not require each source column headers to be exact matches to the below target fields. 
 Mapped Target Field 
 Definition 
 Data Type 
 Core 
 Comments 
 | Visit Name 
 | Planned visit name unique within the study 
 | String 
 | Required 
 | 
 | Visit Order 
 | Visit Sequence Number 
 | Integer 
 | Optional 
 | If not mapped, the system creates a sequence number based on the order of visits in the file 
 | Visit Window Start Days 
 | Number of days that data entry can be done before the target day 
 | Integer 
 | Optional 
 | 
 | Visit Window Target Days 
 | Offset in days after visit is created for ideal data entry 
 | Integer 
 | Optional 
 | 
 | Visit Window End Days 
 | Number of days data entry can continue after the target day 
 | Integer 
 | Optional 
 | 
 | Visit Type 
 | Indicates if visit is planned or unscheduled 
 | String 
 | Optional 
 | By default, all visits are planned. If mapped, the system expects the source record to be: “Unscheduled”, ‘1’, “Yes”, ‘Y’, or, “UNS” for unscheduled visits 
## Sites
 The data source file from the third-party EDC must include a list of sites that can map to the below target fields. The Medidata system does not require each source column headers to be exact matches to the below target fields.

 | The system sets the City to “NA” if Country is not one of: United States, Russian Federation, Australia, Canada 
 The System sets the City to the below values if Country is the following: 
- If the Country is: United States, then City: New York 
- If the Country is: Russian Federation (Code=RUS), then City: Moscow 
- If the Country is: Australia (Code=AUS), then City: Sydney 
- If the Country is: Canada (Code=CAN), then City: Toronto

## Patients
 The data source file from the third-party EDC must include a list of patients and the primary site associated with each patient that can be map to the below target fields. The Medidata system does not require each source column header to be exact matches to the below target fields.

 | Primary Site Number 
 | Primary site associated with the patient 
 | String 
 | Required 
 |

 | Patient ID 
 | String 
 | Required 
 | 
 Note : You cannot create patients associated with more than one site. Clinical Data Studio supports only successfully screened patient data and requires unique patient IDs within the study. Importing duplicate patient IDs causes errors and prevents patient creation in the Medidata platform.
## Connect EDC to Data Connect
## Add Data Source
- Access Data Connect , and click   Imports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click   Add data source . See  Add Data Source for details on how to configure a data source. Use this data source to import metadata. 
## Add Metadata
- On the Data source screen, click Add Metadata > Select the metadata from the list.
- Visits  
- Sites 
- Patients
 Note : These steps use Visit metadata type to define the workflow. The steps to create metadata datasets for the other two metadata type are the same.
- On the Add Visit Dataset screen, click Select source file , and select a file to map the dataset specifications. See Add Dataset for more information. 
- Click Select file.  You can preview the data for the selected file on the right side of the screen. 
- Use the data preview and map the source field to target fields.
 Tip : Data preview contains built-in validation to show errors.
- Click Continue. 
- Define Importing Criteria. For details, see  Add Dataset . 
- Click Add Visits Dataset > Start Import recurrence. 
 Tip : To import the dataset later, select Start Later .
- Repeat for the Sites and Patients metadata.

===== PAGE: import_with_veeva_edc_direct_connect.html | Import with Veeva EDC Direct Connect =====
## Import with Veeva EDC Direct Connect
 You can use Data Connect's Veeva EDC Direct Connect integration to import EDC data by providing API credentials. This data includes metadata dataset extracts and clinical dataset extracts. 
## Limitations and Considerations
## Unsupported CDS Capabilities
 Unsupported Capability
 | Medidata does not support system KRIs ingested from Veeva EDCs:  
- AE Entry Delay 
- Visit Entry Delay 
- Missing Visits 
- Out-of-window Visits 
- System and Non-System Query Rate 
- Non-System Query Delay 
- Number of SAEs per Month 
- Number of AEs per Month 
 | The following other capabilities are not supported from Veeva EDCs: 
- GenAI Audit Trail Review (ATR) 
- Clean Patient Tracker Source Data Verification (CPT SDV) Query Metrics 
- Page Status 
- Weekend and Holiday visits 
- Deep Link into EDC 
## General Limitations
 Category 
 Details 
 | General 
 | 
 Important : Veeva Direct Connect only supports importing data for the latest CRF published version. If there is a new CRF version, all patients must be migrated to the new CRF version to ensure there is no data loss.  
- While you can import multiple form types, Data Surveillance only supports forms with patients, sites, and visits. 
- All third-party EDC studies must be in their own client – that is, completely separate from the Medidata platform – because the system assumes the source of the metadata is Data Connect. For example, if you import sites from Data Connect and use Cloud Administration to update client division organizations (CDOs) and sites, it leads to data inconsistencies. 
- Veeva direct connect does not support ingestion of Veeva LAB module datasets. Any dataset that is produced from Veeva's LAB module will error out ingestion.  
 | Summary of Veeva Direct Connect Gaps and Limitations 
 | 
 Important : There are no plans for Veeva Direct Connect to support Veeva Coder. Instead, you can use Medidata Coder with Veeva datasets. For details on using Coder with external datasets, see  Manage External Verbatims .
- Codelist Decoded 
- Multiple Active eCRF Versions 
- Non-production Study Environment 
- Veeva Query 
- Veeva Lab module datasets
The system rejects any dataset produced from Veeva's Lab module. 
 | Processing Queries in Veeva EDC 
 | The process for submitting and viewing query details for Veeva EDC data mirrors the established workflow for Medidata Rave data. To learn more, see Important Considerations.
- Submit a Query – You can submit queries on non-system fields. You can use either imported raw Veeva EDC data or derived datasets. You can select a single record or multiple records in bulk for query submission. 
- Wait for Synchronization – Veeva EDC generates the query within approximately five minutes. 
- Verify Opened Query in Veeva – You can locate the query with the following corresponding details:
- Source System – CDS_Systems 
- Source User – John Smith (The name of the user who submitted the query in DQM) 
- Source Type – external_v 
- Viewing Query Feedback – Clicking the Status column for an available record retrieves all Veeva EDC queries, including queries created by CDS users and non-CDS users. Veeva provides query details every three hours. 
 Important Considerations 
- System Fields – You cannot submit queries against system fields (for example, SUBJID, SITEID, or system dates). 
- Read-Only Fields – You cannot submit queries against fields designed as read-only within Veeva. 
- Group Selection – You do not need to mark group selection for Veeva EDC data. 
- Query Text Editability – You can only modify query text before submission. 
## Special Considerations 
- Data Connect automatically tags third-party EDC datasets as a Derived Dataset when it requires extensive transformations before being available for activation. 
- Data Connect shows a banner on Data Connect if the onboarding step is not complete.  
- The system checks for new or changed files and processes them every 12 hours.
 Important :
- The Trigger Data Source Synchronization does not trigger a manual refresh for data sources using Veeva Direct Connect. If there is a queue of pending updates, then the system attempts to process the previous update before processing new ones. 
- To use this feature to open EDC queries in Veeva and to retrieve EDC queries from Veeva, the API account user requires the following additional permissions: 
- View Query 
- Open Query 
## Prepare Veeva EDC
 You must have an account in Veeva that can authenticate using a Vault Name, Vault User Name, and Password. This account must have the following permissions:  
- API Access 
- Manage Jobs 
- View Casebook 
- View Study Design 
- View Library 
## Connect Veeva EDC to Data Connect
- Access Data Connect , and click   Imports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click   Add data source . 
- Select Veeva EDC Direct Connect > Next . 
- Provide the below connection details:
- Vault Name – The Veeva vault name  
- Study Name – The Veeva study name 
- User Name – User name to connect to Veeva's API 
- Password – Veeva password 
- Click   Test Connection . 
 Tip : Recheck the connection details if the authentication fails.   You can continue configuring your data source even without testing the connection.  
- Click Add datasource . 
- On the Add clinical dataset screen, select the datasets to import and click Add dataset .
 Note :
- You cannot create patients associated with more than one site. Clinical Data Studio supports only successfully screened patient data and requires unique patient IDs within the study. Importing duplicate patient IDs causes errors and prevents patient creation in the Medidata platform. 
- Data Connect automatically triggers an import from Veeva every 12 hours.  
-  Innovate: 11am and 11pm 
- Production: 12am and 12pm 
- After importing metadata and clinical datasets, the system automatically activates datasets for Data Surveillance. There is no manual activation step required for data sources configured with Veeva EDC Direct Connect.

===== PAGE: imports.html | Imports =====
## Imports
 Imports is a Data Connect capability that facilitates the transfer, validation, and aggregation of external data. You can configure and validate external data transfers based on Data Transfer Agreements. 
## What is Imports?
   Imports is a Data Connect capability with which you can: 
- Establish external data source connections. Imports supports SFTP and Amazon S3 connections. 
- Live stream datasets while adhering to a data transfer agreement (DTA) from the vendor. 
- Monitor the status of the imported datasets. 
- Create multiple versions of the imported data sets for corrections or updates. 
## What Is the Purpose of Imports?
 With Imports, you can process, configure, validate, and transfer external data to clinical programmers, Data Surveillance, and RBQM. You can enhance the data quality without worrying about the complexities of data transfers, monitoring connections, validating files, and preparing the data for clinical programmers.  
## Who Uses Imports?
 Clinical Programmers and Data Managers. 
## How Does Imports Work?
 You can establish an SFTP or S3 connection for external data transfers using imports and monitor the automatic data set transfers. 
 You can configure the data set rules and validations as per the Data Transfer Agreements (DTA). You can monitor the data ingestions, review them, and resolve errors with Imports.
 Important :  Unsupported Data 
 Data Connect does not support variables such as coding dictionaries, coded terms, and code lists. While you can import datasets that includes these data, you cannot see or use them in Data Connect. However, these data can appear as-is in RBQM and Data Surveillance when you activate the associated dataset. For details, see Unsupported Variables in Data Connect . 
## What Are the Benefits of Imports?
 Imports provide:
- An efficient way to automatically ingest external data as close to the raw data as possible. 
- Error recognition in the ingested data. 
## How Do I Access Imports?
 Access Data Connect , and click Imports   in the left-hand menu.

===== PAGE: imports_-_import_external_data_with_data_connect.html | Imports: Import External Data with Data Connect =====
## Imports: Import External Data with Data Connect
## What Is New?
 Data Connect’s Imports capability provides new functionality that manages and imports data from external sources. Users can: 
- Add External Data Sources – Users can set up a data source connection for a study environment using external SFTP and S3 source connections. 
- Add Datasets –
- Users can define a dataset according to the Data Transfer Agreement (DTA) expected from their source systems. 
- The configuration wizard guides users through this process by prefilling the list of expected columns based on a sample file selected by the user. Users can then determine the expected data type and key fields for each column. 
- Users can see a preview of the chosen file to assist with configuration. They can run validation on the column's data type and key fields against the previewed sample data, ensuring accuracy. 
- Users can set import criteria to define the rules for selecting a file to transfer. They can adjust the dataset status to either actively import or pause ongoing imports.  
- This release supports CSV data files. 
- Monitor Processing Status – 
- Users can track an import's progress and the status of imported files. An automated schedule runs every 2 minutes to check for new files in the source folders of all active datasets. The imported file also appears.  
- Users can view the imported records, records with errors, and the reason for any errors when applicable. 
- Edit Datasets with Versioning – 
- After saving a dataset, users can edit its specifications to correct misconfigurations or update definitions in response to source system specification changes. 
- Users can save a dataset as a draft and publish it to create a new version. 
- Edit Import Criteria –
- Users can edit the criteria for selecting a file from source folders even after saving a dataset.  
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 There is no change to the existing data transfer configuration process. These updates augment existing functionalities. Users can now, however, import data from S3 locations.
## Other Applications
- Data Connect > Datasets and Transformations – Users can see the datasets imported with Imports when using Data Connect's Datasets module or the Transformations module. 
- Data Surveillance and RBQM – Users can use transform imported data for use by downstream capabilities such as Data Surveillance and RBQM. 
- Data Connect > Exports – Users can export transformed data using imported external data from Data Connect's Exports module. 
## How Do I Benefit?
 With the ability to:
- Import datasets from external sources, users can use Medidata's risk-based monitoring capabilities such as Data Surveillance with data from their own data sources. 
- Edit import configurations, users can correct misconfigurations or update criteria when file naming conventions are changed in external source systems. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 Users can access these functionalities from Data Connect's Imports tab.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are several knowledge articles that cover how to use Data Connect's Imports module, including: 
- Imports 
- Import External Data with Imports 
## What Else Should I Know?
 When managing import configuration versions o nly one draft can be active at a time, and only one published dataset version can be importing data simultaneously. 
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

## Imports: Automatic Data Type Selection During Dataset Configuration
## What Is New?
 While configuring a dataset using Data Connect's Imports experience, the system automatically detects and selects a data type for users.

## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 There is no change to the existing dataset configuration process . These updates augment existing functionalities. Users can now, however, use the auto-detect functionality to automatically assign data types to the columns. 
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications. 
## How Do I Benefit?
 Earlier, users had to select a data type for each column and navigate between the columns and validation controls, which was a tedious process. Now with auto-detect functionality, the system assigns the data type automatically to each column.

 Users can access these functionalities from Data Connect's Imports tab.

 Users can still select data types for columns manually, and they must manually select the date format for date-based data types.

## Imports: Configure Datasets with Codelists
## What Is New?
 For Data Connect's Imports capability, users can now configure column data type  to have a codelist.  
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All. 
## Workflow Process
 There is no change to the existing  dataset configuration process . These updates augment existing functionalities. Users can now, however, use the Codelist functionality to provide a list of values expected for a specific field.  
## Other Applications
- Data Connect   >   Transformations   – Users can see the data with unique list of values and use them for use cases like unit conversion.  
## How Do I Benefit?
 Users can now define a set of acceptable values for a column, and validate the ingested data. Imports shows an error when the imported file does not meet the acceptable values for the codelist. This eliminates the process of manually validating the imported data for the right format and data. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 Users can access these functionalities from Data Connect's Imports tab. On the Dataset   screen, click the More   icon     >   Edit  to edit the dataset specifications and select Codelist for the data type.

===== PAGE: imports__new_fields_for_decoded_columns_and_standard_units.html | Codelist and Unit Codelist Support for Veeva EDC =====
## Codelist and Unit Codelist Support for Veeva EDC
## What Is New?
 When you import Veeva EDC data with Veeva Direct connect, the system now also imports codelist and unit codelist columns from clinical data files. This enables you to successfully see Veeva EDC data with decoded columns and standard unit columns in Datasets and perform operations on them in Transformations. 
 When you review this data in Data Surveillance, you can configure the KRI, QTL, and Anomaly modules to automatically identify decoded codelist fields from raw fields. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No impact to the workflow process.
## Other Applications
 When you review this data in Data Surveillance, you can configure the KRI, QTL, and Anomaly modules to automatically identify decoded codelist fields from raw fields. 
## How Do I Benefit?
 This change improves Data Connect's compatibility with Veeva EDC such that you retain more data and formatting when you use Veeva Direct Connect, expanding your ability to transform and activate data for Data Quality Management.
## Where Do I See the Change?
- Data Connect > Imports: No changes. 
- Data Connect > Datasets : You can see Veeva data with decoded columns and standard unit columns. 
- Data Connect > Transformations : You can use Veeva data with decoded columns and standard unit columns in your transformations. 
- Data Surveillance : The Codelist column that appears in the KRI, QTL, and Anomaly modules can show these data. 
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are no additional relevant resources.
## What Else Should I Know?
 In Data Surveillance, you cannot issue queries on decoded columns.
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: imports__partial_date_support_for_external_data.html | Imports: Partial Date Support for External Data =====
## Imports: Partial Date Support for External Data
## What Is New?
 You can now configure an imported date or datetime column with partial date. When you import data with partial dates, Imports  interpolates date data for the source date value UNK or UN. You can choose to view or hide the new interpolated date field from all data previews. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No direct impact to existing datasets. However, to enable partial date support for existing datasets, users must upgrade existing datasets to a new version. 
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 The ability to support partial dates provides full transparency for  raw date values at the time of data capture. The system applies rules to populate unknown values and use interpolated d ate or datetime  for analytics. This saves time and mitigates user error caused by manual date interpretation.
## Where Do I See the Change?
 Users can access these functionalities from Data Connect's Imports capability, from the Add Clinical Dataset screen. The Date and Datetime columns no longer show NULL data for partial dates, but instead show interpolated dates. 
## Where Do I Learn More?
 Use the following links to find new and updated content in support of this Enhancement: 
- Add Dataset 
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

## Imports: Support for Versionless Veeva EDC Datasets
 Important:

## Change Details
 Product 
 | Data Connect
 Version 
 | 2026.1.0
 Change Type 
 | Enhancement (Updated Functionality)
 Functionality or Module 
 | Imports > Veeva EDC Direct Connect
 Summary of Changes 
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 Data Connect now uses a version-independent approach to import Veeva EDC data collected across multiple versions at a given time . Imported datasets now appear in a single table featuring a "superset" schema that contains all unique columns from every version of that form . 
 In addition, the system now creates consolidated visits that represent unique visits across all CRF versions found in the event_definitions file
 Benefit to Users 
 | This enhancement simplifies data visibility by consolidating multiple dataset versions into a single, comprehensive view. You can access a superset of data—including all unique columns and consolidated visits—without navigating between different versions or managing upgrades for individual datasets
 Summary of User Impact 
 | 
 Impact to Users and Roles 
 All Data Connect users that use Veeva data sources.
 Impact to Workflow Processes 
 For existing studies, you can now view the latest dataset version for migrated data and future imports. The workflow no longer requires managing multiple versions.
 Impact to Applications 
 No impact to other applications.
 Impact on Interface 
- Data Source View: The Data Source view no longer includes version information to reflect the versionless structure.
 Impact to Existing Data or Settings 
 The system migrated any existing Veeva datasets into new merged versions and deprecated all previous versions. You can no longer use the deprecated projects. 
 User Impact Assessment 
 | High 
 Learning Resource Updates 
 |

 Acronym Glossary

 User Impact Assessment 
 The impact of the change on system behavior and on the user's interaction with it.
- Low – There is no change to current software behavior.
- Moderate – Change includes corrections or improvements that may change user interaction with the software, such as minor UI or minor functionality changes.
- High – Change includes software changes which require review of existing settings, workflow and/or operational procedures to ensure correct user interaction with the software.

 Code Continuity Across Releases
 All Imports releases are cumulative.  That is, each release or software update builds on prior releases as noted in the release notes. - Development 
- Functional Testing 
- Interactive Verification

 User-defined study environments follow in alphabetical order and are case-insensitive.

===== PAGE: include_multiple_studies_in_an_export_job.html | Include Multiple Studies in an Export Job =====
## Include Multiple Studies in an Export Job
## Description
 A single extract job can now extract data from multiple studies of a client division, sharing a single environment. The output varies depending on the extracted data types. 
- For RAW EDC extracts, the number of output files depends on the number of studies, and datasets chosen in each of the studies. 
- With CTMS extracts, the number of output files depends on the number of datasets selected for an extraction, with each dataset containing data from all the selected studies. 
 To simplify the visualization of multiple study export jobs, you can filter the export job list to only show multiple study extract jobs. 
## Affected Roles
 Any user with the Data Extract permission.
## Advantages
 Allows users to extract similar data from all the studies they have access to in a single job.
## User Impact
- Reduces the number of extract jobs a user needs to setup if need to pull the same data from multiple different studies.  
- Data Type is no longer available as an option in the naming of the output zip file. 
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: incremental_data_extraction_now_available.html | Incremental Data Extraction Now Available =====
## Incremental Data Extraction Now Available
## Description
 While creating recurring export jobs to extract CTMS data, you can set the job to extract incrementally. Incremental extracts only contain the added or updated data since the previous run. Incremental extracts do not contain updates about deleted data. The first run of an incremental job is always cumulative. 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Extractor.
## Advantages
 With incremental data extractions, you can select to see only the changes since the last job execution. This saves you time from comparing the previous extract to the current extract to check for data changes.
## User Impact
 The Export Jobs list adds the Export Type column denoting if the job is cumulative or incremental.
 During the New Export Job workflow's Export Data Type step, you can select between Cumulative and Incremental data extracts. Incremental data extraction is only available for the CTMS data types.
 While reviewing incremental data extracts, you can see added or updated data since the job's previous run. Incremental data extracts do not contain updates about deleted data.  
## Special Considerations
 None.

Incremental extracts do not contain updates about deleted data. The first run of an incremental job is always cumulative.

## Advantages
 With incremental data extractions, you can select to see only the changes since the last job execution. This saves you time from comparing the previous extract to the current extract to check for data changes.

===== PAGE: interpolated_variables_behavior_in_data_surveillance_and_rbqm.html | Interpolated Variables Behavior in Data Surveillance and RBQM =====
## Interpolated Variables Behavior in Data Surveillance and RBQM
 In Data Surveillance and RBQM, variables with the suffix_TXT, _INT, _DTC indicate enriched fields from Data Connect.
 Note : Variables in Data Surveillance and RBQM are labelled "fields" in Data Connect. For consistency, this section will use "fields" giong forward.
 Source fields refer to the original source of a data point, while enriched fields add extra information or context to the source data. Clinical Data Studio shows enriched fields to provide more detail about the about source fields, or to add derived information that is not directly present in the original source. If the source dataset fields include unknown values, additional interpolated (enriched) fields with the suffixes _INT, _TXT, etc are added to the source dataset . For example, if the source date is missing the day, the enriched _INT field (integer) interpolates the 1st day of the month, and the _DTC field (datetime) drops the unknown value. The values in the enriched fields differ when dates have unknown (UN or  UNK ) values. If the source field uses dictionary terms, the enriched _TXT field shows the coded terms. For example, AESer and AESer_TXT. 
- Patient Profiles – If you use _TXT values, you see end user strings rather dictionary coded values, that is, No/Yes rather than 0/1 in your patient profile design. If you use a dataset with datetime operations, then you should use the date field and its enrichments, for example: _INT,  DT , _DTC—from the source Rave dataset to ensure data accuracy in the patient profiles. 
- Listings and Data Sets – Interpolated fields such as _INT and _DTC appear as-is in Data Connect. But these fields do not appear for selection in the Datasets pane in Listings as they show as the Rave source field, and are consumed implicitly within the date field. 
- Interpolated fields in derived datasets and derived fields – For Rave-enabled listings containing derived datasets built in Data Connect , you can submit queries only for derived datasets where source fields originate from a Rave dataset. That is, the Submit Query button remains disabled if  the listings contains derived datasets where the source fields does not come from a Rave dataset.  
 Similarly, for Rave-enabled listings containing derived fields, you can submit queries for derived fields  where source fields originate from a Rave dataset.  Otherwise, we recommend you select the form field from the Rave form in the Add Column option, and edit the listing.  
 For details, see  Clinical Data Studio's Data Pipeline .

===== PAGE: introducing_data_connect.html | Introducing Data Connect =====
## Introducing Data Connect
## What Is New?
 Data Connect is a part of Clinical Data Studio. Clinical Data Studio is a complete data management and quality experience that provides seamless access to integrated data from Medidata and non-Medidata sources using AI to streamline data aggregation, standardization, and management workflows. With Clinical Data Studio, clinical or IT programmers, clinical data management, and clinical operations can address data in real time and reduce user burden, shorten operational timelines, increase data quality, and improve safety. 
 Data Connect unifies data discovery, ingestion, transformation, and exports. It provides interoperability across various data sources and systems. Users can explore internal and external datasets, import  non-Medidata data sources, and transform datasets into industry or custom data models. Users can then activate  non-Medidata datasets for downstream Medidata experiences, or export and share datasets to external systems. 
 Extractor is no longer a stand-alone product, but a part of Data Connect . Clinical Data Studio includes rebranded Extractor capabilities along with three other capabilities: Imports, Datasets, and Transformations. 
 You can access Data Connect and other Clinical Data Studio offerings and capabilities from the Medidata home page . Once in Clinical Data Studio, you can select the Data Connect tab to access the following capabilities according to your permissions: 
- Imports – M anage and import data from external sources. 
- Exports – Create and manage export jobs to extract your data from the Medidata platform. 
- Datasets and Transformations – R eview existing datasets, create datasets, and transform them depending on your needs. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All Clinical Data Studio and Extractor users.
## Workflow Process
 You can use Data Connect's capabilities to import, view, transform, and export data.
 Important : Extractor users can continue managing existing export jobs through Data Connect.
## Other Applications
 You can use data imported and transformed with Data Connect in other Clinical Data Studio offerings such as Data Surveillance and RBQM.
## How Do I Benefit?
 With Data Connect you can:
- Ingest Any Data Quickly and Easily – Data Connect provides access to data from Medidata data sources, such as Rave EDC, and simplifies importing data from any  non-Medidata source, such as labs. You can configure data transfer agreements into Data Connect to validate ingested data automatically. 
- Transform Data with Minimal Effort – With Data Connect, clinical data managers build complex listings across multiple datasets without the need for programming. Clinical programmers and biostatisticians use Data Connect's no-/low-code data transformation capabilities to create custom datasets. Data Connect maintains complete traceability of all data transformations so users can always see what and how data was transformed. 
- Extract Data from Various Sources – Users can create export jobs to enable the extraction of data sets from the Medidata platform  for any studies and environments they have access to . 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 You can access Clinical Data Studio on the Medidata Home page, and Data Connect from the Clinical Data Studio's Data Connect tab.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 Use the following links to find new content in support of Data Connect:
- About Data Connect 
- Grant Access to Data Connect 
- Access Data Connect 
- Data Connect Roles and Capabilities 
- Datasets 
- Exports 
- Transformations 
## What Else Should I Know?
 For an historical listing of features introduced into Data Connect's module prior to this release, including beta releases for Datasets and Transformations, Imports, and Exports, see:
- Datasets and Transformations Releases History 
- Imports Releases History 
- Exports Releases History 
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: job_archival_now_available.html | Job Archival Now Available =====
## Job Archival Now Available
## Description
 You can now archive export jobs. Archived scheduled jobs no longer run. You also cannot manually run archived jobs.   
 The Export Jobs screen lists active and archived jobs between the following tabs:
- Active – Shows all active export jobs. 
- Archived – Shows all of your archived export jobs. If you are a super user, this tab shows all archived export jobs within your client division. This tab also includes a Refresh Job List button, in case other users such as a super users or API endpoint users archive your jobs while you are in Extractor. Clicking the button ensures that you see the most current list of archived jobs. 
 You can archive jobs from either the Export Jobs screen or from a specific job's Job Configuration screen.
 Important : You cannot reactivate archived jobs.
## Affected Roles
 All, although only users with the Manage Extracts building block on the platform role can archive jobs.
## Advantages
 You can organize your export jobs by archiving jobs you no longer need. The Archived tab provides an audit trail for archived jobs.
## User Impact
 Existing jobs are generally unaffected by this update. However, if a Super User within your client division has already archived one or more of your jobs, those jobs appear in the Export Jobs screen's Archived tab.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Extractor Knowledge Space provides new and updated content to support the new archival capability: 
- Archive Export (new) 
- Exports Roles and Capabilities (updated) 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: manage_third-party_data_transfers_with_sync.html | Manage Third-Party Data Transfers with Sync =====
## Manage Third-Party Data Transfers with Sync
## What Is New?
 Sync is a part of the Platform Workbench family of capabilities – also known as Data Connect – that augments the workflow of collecting and managing third-party data transfers. With Sync, you can:
- Configure Data Source Connections – You can configure data source connections for a study environment to import data through SFTP.  
- Configure Data Sets – You can configure data import criteria for Sync to identify and use for data transfer. Criteria include validation rules for key fields and column data types. You can manually run validation on a given data type or key field to ensure accurate definitions. 
- Monitor and Manage Data Transfers – You can view a given import's progress and the status of the imported fields. You can also view any imported records, and records with errors and their respective reasons for the errors. 
 Sync integrates with Platform Workbench as a tab within the Platform Workbench's interface.
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 This is Sync's first release, and therefore all functionality is new.
## Other Applications
 You can perform transformations and create derived data sets in Platform Workbench with data you import with Sync.
## How Do I Benefit?
 Sync reduces time spent developing data transfer specifications, data delivery, and issue resolution. With Sync, you can: 
- Configure data source connections such as SFTP, and configure data sets expected from a data source connection. 
- Specify rules through data transfer agreements that Sync enforces during transfer. 
- View data from every source in a single place and resolve data issues as necessary. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 Platform Workbench includes the Import tab where you can access Sync's capabilities.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are no additional relevant resources.
## What Else Should I Know?
 Sync currently supports CSV files.
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: manage_transformation_projects.html | Manage Transformation Projects =====
## Manage Transformation Projects
 With the Transformations experience, you can create and manage transformation projects to serve a variety of use cases. You can create the following types of projects:
- Derived datasets 
- Enriched views such as unit conversions 
- SDTM-like datasets* 
- Custom data models* 
- Custom code projects to host datasets transformed with the Data Connect R SDK* 
 Important : Projects denoted with an asterisk are in limited preview for select customers. 
## Project Considerations
- Projects for SDTM-like datasets, derived datasets, and custom data models include several no-code field-level transformation components, which you can combine to create complex transformations. 
- For complex transformation operations, you can use the Data Connect R SDK Library. For details, see  Generate Custom Code Projects . 
- Errors in a transformation project do not affect the upstream data source or, when applicable, other upstream transformation projects. 
- All users within a study can access any transformation project. However, only one user can configure a project at a time. While a user configures a transformation project, other users concurrently viewing the project only have view access and cannot make changes until the current editor leaves the project configuration screen. 
- Projects have multiple statuses. For details, see  Transformation Project Statuses . 
 Important : While editing an activated derived dataset project, if you make a change without changing the dataset name – such as performing a Replicate operation entering a dataset name – then when you republish the dataset, it creates a dataset with a duplicate dataset name rather than overwrite the original dataset. To fix the issue, do one of the following: 
- To prevent the duplication : R ename the last node to publish a dataset with a new name. 
- If duplication has occurred : Upversion the duplicated dataset, and only activate the dataset that has the highest version . 
## Create Project
 SDTM-like Datasets Enriched Datasets - Unit Conversions Derived Datasets Custom Data Models Custom Code 
 SDTM-like Datasets
 You can use a streamlined no-code process to generate Study Data Tabulation Model
 (SDTM)-like datasets
 across all domains
 , available in version SDTM IG 3.3 and 3.4. This includes CDISC controlled terminology mapping. 
 When you refresh the preview while building data mappings, you can trigger quality checks on demand for your edits based on the SDTM metadata requirements. You can also use Pinnacle 21 Enterprise to validate SDTM datasets.
 Important :  Pinnacle 21 Validation Perquisites 
- You must have a valid Pinnacle 21 enterprise license with Pinnacle 21 version 6.1.1 and above. 
- You must contact your account executive to obtain approval to use the Pinnacle 21 integration. Ensure that you have and share the Domain URL and System/API Key for your Pinnacle 21 implementation 
- To obtain the above details, send an email to  support@pinnacle21.atlassian.net . 
 To create this project:
- Access Data Connect , and click  Transformations  in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Add Project , and enter the following settings:
- Project Name – Enter a project name. 
- Target Setup – Select one of the following:
- Industry Default – Uses the industry SDTM model with no added domains or variables. 
- Custom SDTM-like Target – Prepopulates the dataset with domains and variables based on the selected template. For details, see Publish Projects . 
- Project Type – Select a suitable SDTM version. 
- SDTM Target Template – If using a custom SDTM-like target, select which template to use. 
- Template Version – Select which version to use. 
- CDISC Version – Select required CDISC version to use for the project. 
- Click Add project . 
 For details on configuring this project type, see  Generate SDTM-like Datasets .
 Enriched Datasets - Unit Conversions
 You can configure unit conversion to transform data set fields from one unit into another unit for later mapping. Unit conversion uses a Quantities, Units, Dimensions, and Types (QUDT) industry library to automatically identify relevant source and target units and analytes. The QUDT supports over 1500 units resulting in over 1.5 million pairwise combinations. With unit conversion, you can automatically generate and apply conversion factors, and custom configurations.
 After adding one or more data sets, you can configure unit conversions on each dataset by defining unit fields for their respective unit dataset. This entails:
- Selecting a value for the source dataset. 
- Selecting a unit for the target dataset. 
- Selecting an analyte as necessary. 
 To create this project:
- Access Data Connect , and click  Transformations  in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Add Project , and enter the following settings:
- Project Name – Enter a project name. 
- Project Type – Select Enriched view . 
- Click Add project . 
 For details on configuring this project type, see  2026-07-30_14-28-48_Generate Enriched Datasets with Unit Conversions .
 Derived Datasets
 You can create derived datasets based on a combination of various parameters and using capabilities including: 
- No-code dataset operations, configurations, and transformations 
- The ability to add more variables to the derived dataset 
 With derived datasets, you can combine and transform datasets to support downstream use cases and analytics.
 To create this project:
- Access Data Connect , and click  Transformations  in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Add Project , and enter the following settings:
- Project Name – Enter a project name. 
- Project Type – Select Derived datasets . 
- Click Add project .
 Tip :
- You can also create a derived dataset project from the Datasets tab by clicking Add Dataset > Derived Datasets.  
- To create a derived dataset project from a template, see  View and Manage Templates . 
 For details on configuring this project type, see  Generate Derived Datasets .
 Custom Data Models
 You can generate custom data model templates that you and other users in your study group can reuse across studies. You can create these data models from scratch, from  derived datasets , or from SDTM-like datasets . During configuration, you can use a streamlined no-code process to conform various data sources into the data model's requirements with live data preview and metadata-based validation. 
 To create this project:
- Access Data Connect , and click  Transformations  in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Add Project , and enter the following settings:
- Project Name – Enter a project name. 
- Project Type – Select Custom data model . 
- Available Custom Data Model – Do one of the following:
- Enable, and select a published data model saved as a template . The selected template prepopulates the data model with variables and configurations that you can further update. 
- Disable to start with an empty state. 
- Custom Data Model Version – If using a data model template, you select one of the template's published versions. 
- Click Add project . 
 For details on configuring this project type, see Generate Custom Data Models .
 Custom Code
 Data Connect's Custom Code data project serves as the destination and container for datasets you externally create and transform with Data Connect R Software Development Kit (SDK) . This article covers the different aspects and purposes of the Custom Code project type. 
 To create this project:
- Access Data Connect , and click  Transformations  in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Add Project , and enter the following settings:
- Project Name – Enter a project name. 
- Project Type – Select Custom code (BETA) . 
- Click Add project . 
 For details about this project type, see Generate Custom Data Models .

===== PAGE: map_rave_edc_form_records_with_platform_workbench.html | Map Rave EDC Form Records with Platform Workbench =====
## Map Rave EDC Form Records with Platform Workbench
## Description
 With Platform Workbench, you can create data mappings connecting records from Rave EDC between multiple forms . You can create data maps for specific studies, and then configure those mappings for later use. As your record data during visits, the recorded data appears in Rave EDC according to the mappings you set. 
## Affected Roles
 Select internal users with permission to use Platform Workbench.
## Advantages
 A study's form design can sometimes differ from how you capture data during the study. Data mapping gives you more flexibility reconciling the study design with data capture.
## User Impact
 None. This feature is available to select internal users.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None.
## Special Considerations
 This feature is not generally available and will be used for demo purposes by Medidata personnel.

===== PAGE: map_target_variables.html | Map Target Variables =====
## Map Target Variables
 You can perform source-to-target mapping on variables from a data project's variable mapping screen. You must also map variables when activating a dataset .
 Map by Dragging May by Search Map by Direct Input 
 Map by Dragging
## Map by Dragging
- In the Data Source panel, browse to or search for a data source's dataset.
 Tip : Click the Preview icon to see the fields and data within the form.
- Click the form's Expand icon for a domain’s form, such as Rave > AE, to show its fields. 
- Identify a variable, and click the  Map  button in the Target Mapping and Values column. 
- Drag a field from the Source Dataset panel onto a variable in the right panel using the Drag  icon, for example: AE's AEENDAT field onto the AESPID variable.
 Note :
- The variable must be available for mapping. 
- Once you map a given combination of domain’s field and concept on a form, you cannot map that domain to the same concept on a different form, even if you map a different field.  
- As necessary, click Show More >  Transformation Instruction  to open the Edit Transformation screen, and  configure transformations .
- Repeat steps 1–5 as necessary.
 Tip : After mapping a target variable from a source or through direct input, you can use the target variable as a source for other target variables.
 May by Search
## Map by Search
- In the Source Dataset panel, browse to or search for a domain's form. 
- In the Source Dataset panel, click the Expand icon for a domain’s form to show its fields. 
- Identify a variable, and click the  Enter  button. 
- Enter a field name in a variable’s gray entry field.
 Note : The variable must be available for mapping.
- Select a field from the search results. 
- As necessary, click Show More >  Transformation Instruction  to open the Edit Transformation screen, and  configure transformations . 
- Click the Refresh button in the bottom panel to preview the mapping. 
- Click the Quality Control tab to see any quality issues you must address. 
- Address the quality issues, and repeat steps 1–8 as necessary.
 Tip : After mapping a target variable from a source or through direct input, you can use the target variable as a source for other target variables.
 Map by Direct Input
## Map by Direct Input
- In the Source Dataset panel, browse to or search for a domain's form. 
- In the Source Dataset panel, click the Expand icon for a domain’s form to show its fields. 
- In the SDTM View screen, click  Map  for a domain’s concept, such as Labs, to show its variables. 
- Identify a variable, and click the  Enter  button. 
- Enter a field name in a variable’s gray entry field, such as “SUB_ID”, and press Enter.
 Note : The variable must be available for mapping.
- As necessary, click Show More >  Transformation Instruction  to open the Edit Transformation screen, and  configure transformations . 
- Click the Refresh button in the bottom panel to preview the mapping. 
- Click the Quality Control tab to see any quality issues you must address. 
- Repeat steps 2–9 as necessary.
 Tip : After mapping a target variable from a source or through direct input, you can use the target variable as a source for other target variables.

===== PAGE: medidata_knowledge_hub_provides_a_centralized__transformative_product_content_experience.html | Medidata Knowledge Hub Provides a Centralized, Transformative Product Content Experience =====
## Medidata Knowledge Hub Provides a Centralized, Transformative Product Content Experience
## Description
 The Medidata Knowledge Hub is a new content experience that replaces the current Knowledge Spaces Help in Extractor. The Knowledge Hub intends to unify all customer-facing product knowledge into a single self-service site to give you a streamlined, connected, and personalized interface. The Knowledge Hub allows you to fine-tune your search on certain parameters such as your role or what you are looking for related to your product. 
 The Knowledge Hub puts you, the Medidata user, front and center, and empowers you to self-serve through an innovative, best-in-class knowledge experience integrated with all Medidata products. You do not have to sift through multiple sources of information, but rather enjoy a unified experience, seamlessly integrated with your  product.  
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Knowledge Hub benefits include: 
- A user-friendly design that provides content from all sources in one site. 
- A faceted search that uses product features to refine your results using machine learning and a built-in taxonomy, which is global and consistent. 
- A personalized user experience that lets you define and save your own subset of the content. 
- A fully integrated on-screen user assistance, which gives you quicker time to resolution in a self-service support model.  
- A cross-platform workflow that helps you understand and go through the entire Knowledge Hub. 
## User Impact
 You access the new Knowledge Hub for Ingestor when you click:
- Help > Knowledge Space Home 
- Show More... on applicable interface help guides. 
 When you enter the Knowledge Hub for the first time, you see a series of short tutorials to introduce this new, streamlined experience. 
## Knowledge Updates
 The Knowledge Space contains no new updates. See the new Knowledge Hub at learn.medidata.com . It currently provides the same content as the Knowledge Spaces site and will be expanded with additional content over time.
## Special Considerations
 As of 03 Apr 2023 , the Knowledge Hub is the single source for product knowledge, and the Knowledge Spaces site will be shut down. Any saved links or bookmarks to pages on the Knowledge Spaces may no longer work. You can either: 
- Update your bookmarks, or 
- Use the new “My Collections” feature in the Knowledge Hub to create your own in-hub bookmarks, which you can organize into collections and share with colleagues.

===== PAGE: missing_visit_date_values.html | Missing VISIT_DATE Values =====
## Missing VISIT_DATE Values
 Dataset activation requires you to correctly map variables to carry data over to Data Surveillance. This article explains how to resolve missing VISIT_DATE values in the Target Data Preview. To learn more about mapping variables and target data, see Activate Datasets . 
## Symptoms
 The Target Data Preview does not show the VISIT_DATE variable value. The Source Data Preview shows the value instead. 
## Causes
 You did not map the source VISIT_DATE variable in the top panel of the dataset activation screen. 
## Diagnostic
 Review the top panel of the dataset activation screen to verify if you mapped the source VISIT_DATE variable. 
## Resolution
- Map the source VISIT_DATE variable in the top panel of the dataset activation screen.

===== PAGE: name_outputs.html | Name Outputs =====
## Name Outputs
 After setting the export type for the export, you land on the Outputs screen. From the Outputs screen,  you can define  the names of output files. You can use variables to embed export information into file names.
 Important : You cannot edit or delete after their creation.
## Configure Export Output 
- Enter an export Name.
 Note : 
- Export names can be up to 50 characters. 
- Export names must be unique for each study and environment. 
- Export names can include alphanumeric characters, underscores, and dashes. 
- Select an option specifying the .zip file name for the extracted datasets:
 Select for more ---> Create File Name Dynamically Enter File Name Manually 
 Select for more --->
 Create File Name Dynamically
- You can use variables to build the name of the exported zip file. Drag these variables onto the gray bar to build the output file name.  
- Export ID   –  An internal identifier guaranteed to be unique. This variable is always part of the file name, but you can reposition the variable within the file name. 
- Export Name – The export name. 
- Export Submission Timestamp –   The export's submission time. The time zone appears according to your device's local time settings. 
- Study Name – The study's name 
 Note:
 Variables can create long file names. These long names can cause problems downloading or unzipping the datasets to your local device. 
 Enter File Name Manually
- You can specify a fixed zip file name. Use only alphanumeric characters, underscores, or dashes in the zip file name. 
- Drag elements to form the exported file's dataset names: 
- Export ID – An internal identifier guaranteed to be unique. This variable is always part of the file name, but you can reposition the variable within the dataset name. 
- Export Submission Timestamp –  The export's submission time  
- Environment Name 
- Dataset Name – Th e name of the extracted dataset. This variable is always part of the dataset name, but you can reposition the variable with the dataset name. 
- Study Name – The study's name 
 Note : Variables can create long file names. These long names can cause problems downloading or unzipping the datasets to your local device. 
- Optional . Enable  Export Location to set the export to deliver files to an external location, and enter the external location details:
 Note : Only a user with the proper permissions can configure the location list. For details on setting up external delivery locations, see Configuring External File Connections .
- Select a location.
- In the Path field, enter an extension to the location's path.
When the system delivers files, the files appear in the path extension as shown in the Full Path preview field. 
- Click Create Export to complete the New Export workflow and return to the Exports Screen.
 Tip : You can receive notifications about completed exports through email and the Medidata Message Center . See  View Exports Ready To Download Notifications or  View Exports Failed Notifications for more information.
## Next Step
 Download Data Extracts

===== PAGE: prepare_amazon_s3_bucket.html | Prepare Amazon S3 Bucket =====
## Prepare Amazon S3 Bucket
 The first step to adding an S3 connection is to prepare your S3 bucket to be compatible with Medidata's systems.
 Important : Prerequisites 
- You have an accessible Amazon S3 bucket in your system. If you do not, contact your team’s technical support to create one. 
## Create an AWS S3 Bucket Policy
- Sign into the AWS console for your S3 account. 
- Go to the S3 page, and identify a S3 bucket for the output files. 
- In the S3 bucket’s Security section, update  the following settings:
- Disable Block all public access . 
- Disable Block public and cross-account access to buckets and objects through any public bucket or access point policies . 
- Leave the other options under "Block all public access" enabled. 
- In the Bucket Policy section, select  Edit Bucket Policy to add one of the following policies:
 Important :  Usage Notes 
- Select the policy that corresponds to the Medidata environment you want to grant access to. 
- Replace <bucket-name> and <folder> with the values for your S3 bucket. 
 Innovate Production 
 Innovate

 "Statement": [
 {
 "Sid": "MedidataWrite",
 "Effect": "Allow",
 "Principal": {
 "AWS": "arn:aws:iam::565378680304:role/Hercules-Innovate"
 },
 "Action": "s3:PutObject",
 "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::<bucket-name>/<folder>/*"

 }
 ]
} 
- Save the bucket policy. 
## Create Support Ticket to Link S3 Bucket
- Submit a support ticket to Medidata Support. Provide the S3 bucket name and the name of the study environment that you intend to connect to.
Once submitted, Medidata Support works with the Medidata technical team to connect your S3 bucket to Hercules, Medidata's workflow tool. When the Medidata team completes the necessary steps, they notify you and close the support ticket.

 Add S3 Connection
