===== PAGE: project_capacity.html | Project Capacity =====
## Project Capacity
 Project Capacity measures the amount of system resources used to publish a project in Data Connect Transformations. This feature ensures successful publishing by mitigating the risk of failed publishing occurrences due to resource overuse.
## Overview
 When working in Data Connect > Transformations , the Project Capacity meter show the percentage of resources the current project uses: 
- Maximum Capacity : The maximum project capacity is 100% . 
- Publishing Limits : If a project reaches 100% capacity, you cannot publish the project . An error message appears stating the configuration exceeds the limit and requires a reduction in scope .
 Important : The system exempts existing projects that already exceed the capacity limit as "legacy exceptions." It does not automatically unpublish these projects, but does open them in a Project Lock state. In this state, you cannot add nodes, but can delete node or remove existing operators. 
## Reduce Project Capacity
 If your project reaches maximum capacity, you must modify the project scope to publish it . To reduce capacity usage, you can: 
- Reduce the number of node operations, for example: Join , Group , Replicate or Filter , Pivot , or Unpivot. 
- Decrease the number of derived variables. 
- Split the project into two parts: 
- Reduce the number of node operations in the current project. 
- Publish the current project. 
- Create a second project using the newly published project’s final dataset as the starting source.

===== PAGE: publish_projects.html | Publish Projects =====
## Publish Projects
 After configuring a data project, you can publish it so that others in your study group can see and use its datasets.
## Publication Considerations and Limitations
- While publishing a dataset:
- If the process gets stuck on the In-Progress step, try waiting 5 minutes and then republish the dataset. 
- Resolve all issues identified by the system for the configuration. 
- When publishing a  derived dataset :
- The data lineage must have a single end node to ensure proper versioning. An error occurs if you have more than one end node. 
- A validation error occurs if you try to publish any project created before May 21, 2025 without upgrading the dataset. Before publishing such a project, you must upgrade the dataset in the project. After the upgrade, you can update and publish the project. 
## Publish Transformation Project
 Publish Only Publish and Add to Library Add Published Project to Library

- Otherwise, click  Publish to share this project across the study group.

===== PAGE: rave_ctms_data_objects.html | Medidata CTMS Data Objects =====
## Medidata CTMS Data Objects
 You can extract data pertaining to Medidata CTMS through different export types. The tables below list the data you can download by export type and data objects.
 Important : Your Rave URL configuration can affect your ability to access exported data. For details, please contact your Medidata representative or Medidata Support.
 Note : All data except Study Enrollment Metrics data are available for cumulative and incremental extracts. Study Enrollment Metrics data are only available for cumulative extracts.
 Available data objects:
- CTMS Issues and Actions 
- CTMS Monitoring Visits, Letters, and Reports Data Objects 
- CTMS Documents, Submissions, Enrollment, Milestones, and Service Providers 
- CTMS Tasks 
- CTMS Audit

===== PAGE: rave_ctms_documents__submissions__enrollment__milestones__and_service_providers.html | CTMS Documents, Submissions, Enrollment, Milestones, and Service Providers =====
## CTMS Documents, Submissions, Enrollment, Milestones, and Service Providers
 You can export study management data objects detailing study enrollment metrics and study milestone  dates.  
 Note : Milestones and Study Enrollment Status data are for cumulative and incremental extracts. Study Enrollment Metrics data are only available for cumulative extracts.
 Important : Your Rave URL configuration can affect your ability to access exported data. For details, please contact your Medidata representative or Medidata Support.
## Document Submission Records
 The Document Submissions Records data file provides an overview of document submission records at the site level. 
 Attribute Name 
 Attribute Type 
 Max Length 
 Attribute Description 
 | Document submission record UUID 
 | Unique Identifier 
 | 50 
 | The document submission UUID 
 | Document UUID 
 | Unique Identifier 
 | 50 
 | The Document's UUID 
 | Document Business ID 
 | Unique Identifier 
 | 200 
 | The document's business ID. The business ID is an auto-generated value and begins with the "DOC" prefix. The ID starts at 1 in each study environment. 
 | Document name 
 | Text 
 | 200 
 | The tracked document's name. 
 | Submission UUID 
 | Unique Identifier 
 | 50 
 | The submission's UUID 
 | Submission ID 
 | Text 
 | 200 
 | The submission's system-generated business identification number.

 | Text 
 | 200 
 | The submission's name.

 | 256 
 | The submission's type. 
 | Approving entity Name 
 | Text 
 | 200 
 | The name of the approving entity

 | Unique Identifier 
 | 50 
 | The approving entity's UUID 
 | Approving entity type 
 | Text 
 | 200 
 | The type of approving entity 
 | Approving entity type UUID 
 | Unique Identifier 
 | 50 
 | The UUID of the approving entity type 
 | Planned Document submission date 
 | Date 
 | 200 
 | The planned submission date. Study and country level submissions define today based on the UTC timezone. Site level submissions define today based on the site's local timezone. 
 | Actual Document submission date 
 | Date 
 | 200 
 | The actual submission date. This value cannot be in the future or after the Actual Submission Approval value. Study and country level submissions define today based on the UTC timezone. Site level submissions define today based on the site's local timezone. 
 | Planned Document submission approval date 
 | Date 
 | 200 
 | The submission's planned approval date. Study and country level submissions define today based on the UTC timezone. Site level submissions define today based on the site's local timezone. 
 | Actual Document submission approval date 
 | Date 
 | 200 
 | The submission's actual approval date. This value cannot be in the future or before the Actual Submission Date value. Study and country level submissions define today based on the UTC timezone. Site level submissions define today based on the site's local timezone. 
 | Document submission approval status 
 | Text 
 | 50 
 | The submission's status.

 | 200 
 | User who linked document to submission

 | Date 
 | 200 
 | The date and time when someone entered details about the submission.

 | Date 
 | 200 
 | The date and time when someone updated details about the submission.

 | Text 
 | 200 
 | The user name who updated details about the submission. 
 | Deleted 
 | Text 
 | 50 
 | Denotes if the submission is deleted.

===== PAGE: rave_ctms_issues_and_actions.html | CTMS Issues and Actions =====
## CTMS Issues and Actions
 You can export CTMS Issues and Actions data objects detailing issues , actions, and comments data. The data include protocol deviation s and their associated action items for subject s, sites , and studies as reported in Medidata CTMS . 
 Note : All data are for cumulative and incremental extracts.
 Important : Your Rave URL configuration can affect your ability to access exported data. For details, please contact your Medidata representative or Medidata Support.

===== PAGE: rave_ctms_monitoring_visits__letters__and_reports_data_objects.html | CTMS Monitoring Visits, Letters, and Reports Data Objects =====
## CTMS Monitoring Visits, Letters, and Reports Data Objects
 CTMS Monitoring Visits, Letters, and Reports data objects contain visits, letters, and report data related to site monitors' activities. The data include visit report submissions and comments, and contact details made with the sites across studies. 
 Note : All data are for cumulative and incremental extracts.
 Important : Your Rave URL configuration can affect your ability to access exported data. For details, please contact your Medidata representative or Medidata Support.

===== PAGE: rave_dataset_versioning_and_scenarios.html | Rave Metadata Versioning and Scenarios =====
## Rave Metadata Versioning and Scenarios
 With Data Connect, you can view and perform actions on datasets imported from Rave. Data Connect tracks changes to the dataset's metadata, which can impact downstream applications that consume Rave data. The flexibility of Rave form structuring and for a potentially infinite number of dataset versions and data scenarios. Because of this, this article highlights two common metadata change patterns and how Data Connect represents these changes as dataset versions so you can interact with the data that you need. 
## What Triggers Metadata Changes?
 When a Rave user changes any eCRF form in the dataset, it changes the form's metadata. This changes the metadata for the entire dataset, even if there was only a single change to a single field on a single form. Changes that can trigger metadata changes can include:
- Changes to a field's data type, data format, name, or ordinal (position) 
- Changes to the codelist 
 Data Connect tracks these changes as dataset versions that you can view from the Datasets experience. In Data Connect, the dataset's version increments even when a user changes a field and reverts the change. 
 Important :
- Data Connect only supports published eCRFs. 
- If a user only changes the fields of a form, the user can choose to not update the  form version in the Rave context. This has implications in the Data Connect context, highlighted below. 
- You can see all eCRF versions from Rave, but to get the correct record count when performing transformations, you must:
- Perform a union combining all eCRF versions; or, 
- Ensure that you migrate all patients into the latest eCRF version. 
## Grouping for Dataset Versions
 In Rave, when you choose to create a form version, the system applies the new version at the study level. The system in turn applies the new version to every form in the study , even those that have not been changed. 
 When you view the Rave dataset in Data Connect's Datasets experience using the Data Preview capability, you can select between multiple dataset versions in the Version menu. Because dataset versions can increment when users update forms and revert those updates, there can be multiple dataset versions that have the same metadata version, that is: they have the same semantic meaning. 
 The Version menu groups these semantically identical dataset versions together as a single menu item. Dataset versions that have true changes – that is, those that have different metadata attributes – appear as separate menu items.
 In the above example, the first set of dataset versions – 4958 and 5131 – have the same semantic meaning, and Data Connect therefore treats it as a single dataset version. Likewise the second set of dataset versions – 2129, 2296, ... 2730 – have the same semantic meaning compared to themselves and a different semantic meaning compared to the first set, and thus Data Connect lists the second set as a second menu item. This grouping continues with the third set, fourth set, and so on.
 Important : When you do not update the form version after changing it in Rave, the dataset still changes in the Data Connect database. The dataset version number remains the same, but the position of that dataset version moves to a set with the same semantic meaning. For example, the dataset version can move from the second menu item to the third item, if it is similar to the other versions in that set; or to its own set. If the user does update the eCRF form, then the dataset upversions as expected, and gains an entry on the Versions menu. 
## Versioning for Datasets with Removed Fields
 Rave users can inactivate or delete form fields when they no longer need them. In Data Connect, if these removed fields, represented as columns in the dataset table, have historical data, they remain in the data preview.  This is to ensure persistence in the data catalog – adhering to FAIR principles – and to minimize breaking checkings in downstream workflows and applications. 
 Data Connect handles the dataset differently depending how a user removed the field in Rave:
- Inactivated fields – The corresponding columns show NULL data for new patients. 
- Deleted fields – Removing the field changes the metadata of the dataset. If you decide to create a new form version in Rave, then Data Connect adds a dataset version in the Version menu. The new dataset version represents the change with the removed column, but Data Connect's data preview continue to shows the column.

===== PAGE: rave_edc_data_extract_file_type_differences.html | Rave Data Extract File Type Differences =====
## Rave Data Extract File Type Differences
 You can configure exports to export data files as comma-separated values (CSV) files, as  SAS V5 Transport files (XPT), or as JSON files. The extracted data is identical between both file types, but the file structure differs. This article describes differences between the CSV and XPT format for the Rave (RAW) data type . 
 For details on extracting data, see  Extracting Data with Exports . 
 Note : SAS V5 Transport file export is only available when exporting Rave RAW data. When you select the SAS file type for Rave RAW data, the exported file outputs as a compressed file containing both CSV and XPT files. 
 Summary
 Description 
 CSV
 XPT
 | Field Name Length Limits 
 | Field names can differ between the file types. 
 | Matches Medidata standards
 | Limited to 8 characters
 | Field Type Changes
 | Fields containing a combination of characters and numbers can appear in different formats between the file types. 
 | Numeric format
 | String format
 | Extra Data Columns
 | The extracted files can have extra data columns containing applicable metadata related to the file type. 
 | No added columns
 | Added columns:
- SAS_ALTERED_ITEM_NAME – Shows mappings for any fields truncated in the XPT file. 
- SAS_FORMAT – Shows the formatting applied to the CSV file when generating the XPT file. 
 | Datetime Field Format
 | The export process treats datetime-based fields as dates and applies the Date format in extracted files. However, the export process treats date-based fields as characters and applies the Char format in extracted files. The extracted files can have extra columns containing applicable metadata related to the date field. 
 | No added columns
 | Added Columns: 
- _DT 
- _DTC 
- _DTI 
 You can disregard the content of these columns.
 | Time Field Format
 | Time-based fields do not have a standardized format. The export process treats time-based fields as strings and applies the String format in the extracted files. 
 | String format 
 | String format

===== PAGE: rave_edc_data_objects.html | Rave Data Objects =====
## Rave Data Objects
 Rave data objects contain raw electronic case report form (eCRF) data from Rave. While configuring exports, you set which Rave data to extract.
 Important : Your Rave URL configuration can affect your ability to access exported data. For details, please contact your Medidata representative or Medidata Support.
## Extract Expectations by Field Type
 Numeric 
 Appears as entered in Rave. 
 Unit Dictionary 
 Appears as entered in Rave.
 Character 
 Appears as entered in Rave.
 Data Dictionary 
 Appears as entered in Rave.
 Date 
 The extracted format changes depending on the field's format in Rave: 
- ISO 8601 Date – Formatted in Rave as <Field_OID>_DT. If the original date value is a complete date, then the extracted date value appears in the ISO 8601 format. 
- Strongly Typed Date – Formatted in Rave as <Field_OID>_DTC. The extracted date value appears in the YYYY-MM-DD format. If the date is a partial date, then "–-" appears for the missing values. 
- Interpolated Date – Formatted in Rave as <Field_OID>_DTI. The extracted date value appears in the ISO 8601 format. If the date is a partial date, then the extracted date is interpolated. For values missing in Rave, the extracted file makes the following substitutions:
- If the day is missing, then the extracted file substitutes "01" for the day. 
- If the month is missing, then the extracted file substitutes January as the month. 
- If the year is missing, then the extracted file does not show the date. 
 Scenario 
 eCRF Field in Rave 
 ISO 8601 Date 
 Strongly Typed Date 
 Interpolated Date 
 | Complete Date 
 | 04 APR 2015 
 | 2015-04-04T00:00:00 
 | 2015-04-04 
 | 2015-04-04T00:00:00 
 | Partial Date: Missing Day 
 | UNK MAR 2006 
 | 
 | 2006-03--- 
 | 2006-03-01T00:00:00 
 | Partial Date: Missing Month 
 | 15 UNK 2006 
 | 
 | 2006---15 
 | 2006-01-15T00:00:00 
 | Partial Date: Missing Year 
 | 
 | 
 | 
 | 
 Medical Coding 
 For fields coded with Coder, verbatim terms for coding decisions appear in the <Field_OID>RAW field. Likewise, preferred termed appear in the <Field_OID>PT field. When coding decisions are unavailable, values for preferred terms are automatically loaded by the system.

===== PAGE: rerun_data_extracts.html | Rerun Data Extracts =====
## Rerun Data Extracts
## Description
 MEDS Extractor allows you to rerun previously created export jobs. 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with Data Export permissions.
## Advantages
 Existing export jobs can be re-executed on demand, so y ou do not need to create another export job to repeat a given data export. 
## User Impact
 From the list of existing data export jobs, you can select  Run Now  from  for a given data export.  This re-executes the job immediately. 
## Special Considerations
 You can re-run an export job if you created the job and  the job is not currently running.

===== PAGE: rerun_export.html | Rerun Export =====
## Rerun Export
 You can rerun a previously completed export to get the latest data instead of creating an export. You can rerun an export from the Exports screen. After rerunning the export, a new instance of the export appears on the Export Run History screen. Once the export completes, you can download the extracted data files. For details, see Download Data Extracts . 
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu to land on the Exports screen.
 Note : You can only see the exports you created unless you have Super User permission that allows you to see all exports in the study group. For details, see Data Connect Roles and Capabilities . 
- Identify an export to rerun, and click the Options icon >  Run Now .
 Note : 
- You can only select Run Now for the exports you created. You cannot execute exports created by another user. 
- You cannot select Run Now when the export is "In Progress."

===== PAGE: revamped_data_project_creation_and_review_capabilities.html | Revamped Data Project Creation and Review Capabilities =====
## Revamped Data Project Creation and Review Capabilities
## Description
 Platform Workbench has been rebuilt to expand your ability to create and manage data projects in the ways outlined in the below table: 
 Workflow 
 Capability 
 | Project Creation 
 | 
 You can:
- Name the project. 
- Select a target data set standard for the project, and the data set standard's version.
 Note : The current available standard and version is SDTM version 3.3.
 | Reviewing the Project List 
 | You can:
- Update or archive projects. 
- Select a study and view its data sources, such as Rave EDC. When you select the study, you can: 
- See a summary of that study’s available data projects as a table that includes the following metadata about each data project: 
- Target view 
- Constituent data sources 
- Creation date 
- Project Status – not started, ongoing, published, or archived 
## Affected Roles
 Select internal users with permission to use Platform Workbench.
## Advantages
 Platform Workbench serves as end-to-end data standardization tool using RAVE EDC as sources, and converting them into Adverse Event (AE) and Vital Signs (VS) SDTM as target data sets. With the expanded project creation options, you can build projects that help reconcile the study design with data capture. 
## User Impact
 None. This feature is available to select internal users.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None.
## Special Considerations
 This feature is not generally available and will be used for demo purposes by Medidata personnel. 
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to RCM.

===== PAGE: revamped_source_to_target_mapping_and_transformation.html | Revamped Source to Target Mapping and Transformation =====
## Revamped Source to Target Mapping and Transformation
## Description
 When configuring data mappings, you can: 
- Choose forms and fields to map, and drag to target fields of the available concepts. 
- Configure custom transformation logic in PostgreSQL (postgres) code to source fields while applying them to the target variable. 
- Review data lineage between source fields into the target variables. 
## Affected Roles
 Select internal users with permission to use Platform Workbench.
## Advantages
 The expanded data mapping options give you more flexibility reconciling the study design with data capture. 
## User Impact
 None. This feature is available to select internal users.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None.
## Special Considerations
 This feature is not generally available and will be used for demo purposes by Medidata personnel.
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to RCM.

===== PAGE: review_data_extracts_in_meds_extractor.html | Review Data Extracts in MEDS Extractor =====
## Review Data Extracts in MEDS Extractor
 Note : This feature appeared as part of MEDS Extractor 2019.1.0.
## Description
 MEDS Extractor allows you to review the progress of extracts, and to download completed extracts to a local device.  You can review the current status of all data extracts  for all studies across environments.
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Reviewing data extracts allows you to:
- Review the success of exported data so you can take further action. 
- Download the exported data to your local drives for further processing. 
## User Impact
 In MEDS Extractor, you can select an existing extract to review its progress. While reviewing the progress of an existing extract, you can:
- Review extracts across other studies and environments. 
- Download exported data to your local drives whilst it is available for download. 
## Special Considerations
- Extracts are available for download to your local device up to 24 hours after completion.

===== PAGE: review_datasets.html | Review Datasets =====
## Review Datasets
 From the All Datasets screen, you can see a list of accessible datasets within your study group and their details.
## Review the Datasets List
 The datasets list shows various details about your datasets. 
 You can filter the dataset list by clicking one of the following categories:
- All – Show all datasets. 
- Alerts  – Show all dataset that has errors or warnings. 
- Internal – Show datasets from internal sources from within the Medidata platform, such as Rave data. 
- SDTM / Enriched View / Custom Data Model / Derived Dataset – Show datasets from the respective data transformation type as produced through Data Connect's Transformations capability. 
- External – Show datasets from sources imported through Data Connect, such as Labs data. 
- Sensor – Show datasets from the Sensor Cloud . 
 Column 
 Description 
 | Label 
 | The dataset's name as set from its source.
 | Collection 
 | The dataset's collection. For more about dataset collection, see  Manage Dataset Collections .
 | Type 
 | The dataset's type, such as Rave, Derived Dataset, or SFTP Data Source.
 | Source 
 | 
 The dataset's data source, such as Rave or the data source specified when configuring an imported source or transformed source.
 Note : All datasets are live from their respective sources, and are, when applicable, automatically transformed based on their respective transformation configurations.
 | Activation 
 | 
 Denotes if the dataset is Activated or Inactive. Other compatible Medidata capabilities use Activated datasets for their functionalities, such as Data Surveillance . For more about dataset activation , see  Activate Datasets . 
 | Status 
 | 
 Denotes the centralized status for the dataset. You can view if the consolidated dataset status with the module that the dataset gave an error or warning. 
 | Last Updated 
 | Denotes the most recent date in MMM DD, YYYY format when the dataset's updated configuration is published. Usually, if this field is empty, then it means that its source is live-streaming the records. To learn more, see Dataset Statuses . 
 Important : Data Latency Timeline 
 Because the data can come from different sources, they can have different refresh cycles. For example, Rave data can take up to 12 hours to appear after loading them the first time. For details, see  Dataset Latency .
## Review a Specific Dataset
 Clicking a specific dataset label opens the dataset's details. Depending on the dataset, you can perform different actions accessible by different tabs.
 Tip : Click anywhere on a specific dataset row other than the dataset label to view dataset status for Data Connect. 
 Tab 
 Description 
 | Data Preview 
 | Shows a table of the dataset's variables and records. You can hide columns and filter data as necessary. 
 | Profiling 
 | You can profile your dataset with various filters and fields. For details about dataset profiling, see  Dataset Profiling . 
 | Activation 
 | Shows the dataset's activation status. You can activate a dataset, or deactivate or edit a deactivated dataset. For details about dataset activation, see  Activate Datasets . 
 | Unregistered Values 
 | Shows a list of the dataset's records that have not been registered to the study. 
 By registering values, you can check and map visits from derived datasets or external datasets to existing visits. You can also create unscheduled visits and map visits to those as well.
 Important : The system consolidates Rave dataset versions that have the same metadata. You can select between dataset versions from the Versions menu. For details, see  Rave Metadata Versioning and Scenarios .

===== PAGE: review_export_configuration.html | Review Export Configuration =====
## Review Export Configuration
 After   creating an export , you can review the export's configuration details from the Export's Settings screen.
## Open the Export Configuration Screen
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- On the Exports screen, select an export to open the Export.
- Click the Settings tab to view export configurations. 
 Note :  You can only see the exports you created. You cannot see exports created by other users unless you have the Super User permission. For details, see Data Connect Roles and Capabilities . 
## Export Configuration Details
## Datasets
 Export Level 
 Denotes if the export extracts at the study level or at the client level:
- Study Level – The export extracts data across selected studies. For exports at this level, this field also shows the export's target study 
- Client Level – The export extracts data across all studies within the client. For exports that this level, this field also shows the export's target client division 
 Environment 
 The export's target study environment. This field only appears for exports created at the study level.
 Data Source 
 The data source from which the datasets are extracted.
## Schedule
 Frequency 
 The export's run frequency. For details on schedule configuration, see Schedule Export .
 Run Start Date 
 The export's execution start time and date. This field appears even if you chose the Run Immediately frequency. For details on schedule configuration, see Schedule Export .
 The Medidata platform stores the date in UTC, and shows the date based on your device's local time. 
 Run End Date 
 If selected during configuration, the export's execution end time and date. This field only appears for exports with the Run Once (Scheduled) or Repeats frequencies. For details on schedule configuration, see Schedule Export .
 The Medidata platform stores the date in UTC, and shows the date based on your device's local time.
 Pause/Resume Export Schedule 
 For recurring exports, you can pause or resume the export, clicking the Pause Export Schedule button pauses the export. For paused exports, the button turns into Resume Export Schedule. Clicking Resume Export Schedule resumes the recurring export. 
## Data Source
 Export Level 
 Denotes if the export extracts at the study level or at the client level:
- Study Level – The export extracts data across selected studies. For exports at this level, this field also shows the export's target study 
- Client Level – The export extracts data across all studies within the client. For exports that this level, this field also shows the export's target client division. 
 Environment 
 The export's target study environment. This field only appears for exports created at the study level.
 Created By 
 The name of the user that created the export or version.
 Date Created 
 The export's creation date. The Medidata platform stores the date in UTC, and shows the date based on your device's local time.
 Data Type 
 The export's target data types. 
 Export Type 
 The export's export type:
- Cumulative –  Each export instance exports all available data. 
- Incremental – Each export instance only exports data changes since the previous run. The first incremental run is cumulative. 
 File Type 
 The file type of the export's constituent extracted files:
- CSV – Comma-separated value 
- XPT – SAS file format 
 Version 
 The export's version number. This field shows "1" for new exports. 
 Modified by 
 The name of the user that modified the export or version.
 Modified Date 
 The export version's modification date. This field reflects when the export changed from version x to version x+1 and shows "-" for new exports. 
## Output Connection
 Configuration Details 
 If configured, this field describes the SFTP or FTPS or S3 location where the export copies the extracted files. Otherwise, the field shows "Not Configured".

===== PAGE: review_export_run_history.html | Review Export Run History =====
## Review Export Run History
 After  creating an export , you can review  a table of the export's completed executions from the Export run history screen. 
## Open the Export Run History Screen
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- On the Exports screen, select an export.
 Note : You can only see the exports you created. You cannot see exports created by other users. 
- Click Export run history tab.
## Export Run History Details
 Start Time 
 The run's start date and time. The Medidata platform stores the date and time in UTC, and shows the date and time based on your device's local time.
 End Time 
 The run's completion date and time. The Medidata platform stores the date and time in UTC, and shows the date and time based on your device's local time.
 Duration 
 The length of time the run took to complete.
 Version 
 The export's version number.   Clicking the version takes you to that version's configuration details.  
 Status 
 The export's final status – Success or Failed.
 Error 
 This shows the error details if the export fails. See  Troubleshoot Common Errors for Failed Exports for details.

===== PAGE: review_exports_list.html | Review Exports List =====
## Review Exports List
 The Exports screen lists the exports that export datasets from the Medidata Platform.  You can review the status of each export, and download datasets from successfully completed exports.
## Open the Exports List
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
 Note : By default, you can only see the exports you created. To see exports created by other users, your platform role must include the View as Super User building block. For details on managing permissions, see Grant Access to Data Connect . 
## Exports List Details
 Tips :  Sorting and Filtering the Exports List 
- Clicking any column header sorts the list by that field. The sorting toggles between ascending and descending. 
- Clicking the Filter icon reveals filtering options. You can filter the exports list by: Frequency, Export Type, Last Run Status, Enrivonrments, Export, Data Type, Export Activity, Created by or, Study View. You can also select  View Multi Study Environment Only to filter the export list to only show multistudy exports. 
 Name 
 The export's name. 
 Export 
 The study or client division the export is exporting from.
 Data Type 
 The export's target data types.
 Last Run Status 
 The export's most recent execution status.
 Next Run 
 The export's next execution start date. If this field is empty, then the export runs immediately, or all scheduled exports are complete. For details on schedule configuration, see Schedule Export.
 Created By 
 Shows the name of the user who created the export.
 Note : This column only appears for users with the View as Super User building block applied to their platform role.
 Job-specific Actions 
 By clicking the More menu , you can perform actions to a specific export, including:
- View Export Details – Takes you to the export's Configuration screen. For details, see  Review Export Configuration . 
- Edit   – Opens the export wizard where you can edit the export parameters and create another version. For details, see Edit Export 
- Run Now – Executes the export immediately, and creates another entry on the export's Run History screen. For details about the Export Run History screen, see Review Export Run History . 
 Note : 
- You can only select Run Now for the exports you created. You cannot execute exports created by another user. 
- You cannot select Run Now when the export is "In Progress." 
- Download Latest Run – If available, downloads the extracted data files for the most recent successful export run. The download option is only available for 24 hours after completed successful run. For details, see Download Data Extracts . 
- Pause Export  – If the export is recurring, pauses the repeat schedule. 
- Resume Export  – If the export is recurring but paused, resumes the paused repeat schedule. 
- Archive Export – Archives the export. For details, see  Archive Export .

===== PAGE: revised_permissions_for_export_jobs.html | Revised Permissions for Export Jobs =====
## Revised Permissions for Export Jobs
## Description
 MEDS Extractor permissions have been extended to permit data extraction  from  Study Management , Issue Management, and Site Monitoring.  Additionally, the permissions to extract clinical data from EDC have been updated.  
 To create a job to extract data you need to have  Manage Extracts permission and access to at least one study site. 
 You also need the specific permission for the type of data to be extracted 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the Admin Standard Role. 
## Advantages
 The updated export job permissions allow you to fine-tune the data you can extract. 
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, you can add the following permissions to your customer-defined platform role: 
- Manage Extracts   – Allows you to create export jobs. Manage Extract was formerly the Extract Data permission. 
- Extract EDC Data   – Allows  you extract EDC clinical data. 
- Extract CTMS Data   – Allows you to extract any of the CTMS data types. 
 To create or manage export jobs then the overall Manage Extracts permission is required for a user.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: sample_derived_variable_calculations.html | Calculations to Output Unsupported Derived Variable Operations =====
## Calculations to Output Unsupported Derived Variable Operations
 Transformations do not support the time fields with format HH:MM. However we can still calculate the time difference using the below procedure.
 HH:MM Time Format Absolute Value Operator 
 HH:MM Time Format
 While configuring a derived variable project, go to the List View and do the following:
- From the target variable list screen, click the More icon, select  Add Variable,  and enter the below details:
- Alias : Date 01 
- Description : Date 01 
- Variable Data Type : Date  
- Variable Format : YYYY MM DD HH:MM 
- Target Variable Requirement : Required 
- Click Add Variable. 
- Repeat step one to add one more variable and enter the below details.
- Alias : Date 02 
- Description : Date 02 
- Variable Data Type : Date  
- Variable Format : YYYY MM DD HH:MM 
- Target Variable Requirement : – Required
 Important : Make sure that the Variable format for both the variables must be same. 
- For variable Date_01 and Date_02, click  Select target variables  and chose the applicable date variables from the list.
 Note :  For this example we are selecting  TXTM   and  TXETM   variables for Date_01 and Date_02 respectively.   
 Absolute Value Operator
 From the Create Transformations screen, use the modules to extract the absolute value:
- Convert the column into a string. 
- Use the Extract Number function on the string.

===== PAGE: sas_export_file_support_for_data_extracts.html | SAS Export File Support for Data Extracts =====
## SAS Export File Support for Data Extracts
## Description
 You can choose to export data files in the SAS file format(*.xpt). Extractor exports to the   SAS V5 Transport file format. 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 You can have more options to download your clinical data, avoiding possible issues when converting from the comma-separated value (*.csv) format to the SAS format. In addition, sponsors can convert the exported(*.xpt) files into Simplified TS (ts.xpt) files to meet data submission requirements for regulatory authorities.
## User Impact
 When configuring export jobs, you can set the exported file type to.xpt. You can only export data of the EDC (RAW) type to SAS format.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Extractor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support the new file type: 
- Extracting Data with Exports 
- Select Datasets 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: schedule_data_extracts.html | Schedule Data Extracts =====
## Schedule Data Extracts
## Description
 You can schedule data exports  to run at a future date and to repeat at specified intervals.
## Affected Roles
 Roles with Data Extract permissions.
## Advantages
 Scheduling data set exports simplifies routine extraction of data from MEDS  as it removes the need for regular manual interactions.
## User Impact
 You can schedule exports at a specific time of these date options: 
- Run Once.  (At a specified future date and time) 
- Every day. 
- Weekly on selected day(s) of the week. 
- A specific date of the month. 
- The last day of the month. 
## Special Considerations
 Extracted data  are only available for 24 hours after a scheduled export job has run.

===== PAGE: schedule_export.html | Schedule Export =====
## Schedule Export
 After selecting or reviewing datasets extract , you land on the export's Schedule screen. You can set exports to run immediately, schedule exports to run at a future date, or schedule exports to repeat at a regular frequency.  
 Repeating options differ by data type. For a table comparing the data types, see  Export Configuration Compatibility by Data Type . 
## Configure Export Run Schedule
- From the Schedule screen, select a schedule for export execution: 
- 
 Run Immediately 
 The export only runs once, and executes now. Exports run immediately by default. 
- 
 Run Once (Scheduled) 
 The export runs once at the selected time. Exports stores the date and time as UTC, and shows the date and time in your local time.
- Select a date and time in to run the export. 
- 
 Repeats 
 The export repeats that the defined interval:
- Minutely – The export runs each minute, starting from the start date and time.
 Note : The Hourly frequency is only available for the Data Connect data type.
- Hourly – The export runs each hour, starting from the start date and time.  The hour menu starts at the upcoming hour.
 Note : The Hourly frequency is only available for the Audit or CTMS data types.
- Daily – The export runs at the selected hour each day, starting from the start date.  The hour menu starts at the upcoming hour. 
- Weekly  – The export runs on the selected hour on the selected days of every week.  The export first executes on the next selected day and hour on or after the scheduled start date. 
- Monthly  –   The export runs at the selected date and hour each month. exports only run  on the months containing that date. 
- Last day of month  –   The export runs at the selected hour on the last day of each month. This option ensures that exports run on the last day of every month, irrespective of the number of days in the month.
 Important : There are no options to delete exports. Therefore, select an end date for any repeating export.
- Click Continue to go to the Data Export Type screen. 
## Next Step
 Set Data Export Type

===== PAGE: sdtm-like_data_set_creation_and_standardization.html | SDTM-like Data Set Creation and Standardization =====
## SDTM-like Data Set Creation and Standardization
## What Is New?
 You can perform source-to-target data mapping and transformations for all Study Data Tabulation Model (SDTM) domq∂Ωains within a selected SDTM version. With this feature, users can: 
- View source data records and fields during SDTM-like data generation 
- Configure SDTM target variables by mapping from source data fields, current target domain variables, and entered values 
- Customize transformations using no-code modules for field transformation 
- Configure supplemental variables; that is, variables that are not part of the SDTM domain 
- Preview results of the SDTM-like data set during configuration 
- Validate the results of the SDTM-like data set during configuration based on the implementation guide's SDTM domain metadata requirement 
 The field transformation module includes:
- Over 20 no-code modules including mathematical functions, conditional functions, and concatenation 
- The ability to create nested transformation functions 
- Custom code support for open source postgres SQL 
 When you refresh the preview while building data mappings or derived data sets, Platform Workbench performs quality checks on your edits. Platform Workbench bases these checks on the data catalog’s SDTM metadata requirements. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All users.
## Workflow Process
 You can perform data mappings and transformations for data projects created with the Select from Existing Target Views option. 
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 By transforming data using the modules, you  can complete up to 80% of SDTM-like data standardization tasks even if you are a nontechnical user. The automated results preview and live validation reduce iteration and hand-off points during the data standardization process. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 You can see source data by clicking the Preview button on the left panel. While configuring an SDTM-like data set, a Refresh button appears in the Data Preview tab. Once published, you can see your published data sets on the Study Projects screen > Data Source section > Curated Views list.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 The Platform Workbench Publication provides new content to support this feature:
- Generate SDTM-like Datasets 
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: select_data_source.html | Select Data Source =====
## Select Data Source
 To create an export, you must first select the data source you plan to export datasets from. You must decide what data types to export, and which studies and which environments to export from.
## Select Data Source
 You can start the export creation from the Exports screen.
- Access Data Connect , and click   Exports   in the left-hand menu. 
- Click Create export . 
- On the Data Source wizard, select the extraction level.
- Select one or more data types.
 Note : 
- Your role determines the data types that appear in the Data Types menu. For details, see  Data Connect Roles and Capabilities . 
- When you select t he Master Data type, you cannot select either of the Audit Master Data or Audit CTMS data types. In addition, when you select the Audit Master Data type, you cannot select any other data type. Similarly, when you select the Audit CTMS data type, you cannot select any other data type. 
- For study level exports, select one or more Studies and an Environment; otherwise, skip to step 7.
 Note: The environments list sorts environments by importance. 
- Production 
- Development 
- Functional Testing 
- Interactive Verification 
- User Acceptance Testing (UAT) 
- Training 
- Other.   Custom study environments appear in alphabetical order, and are case-insensitive. 
- Click  Continue to go to the Select Datasets screen. 
 Note : There can be intermediate steps between the Select Data Source step and the Select Datasets step for some export types, such as Data Connect (Transformed Data). In those cases, follow the prompts until you reach the Select Datasets step. 
## Next Step
 Select Datasets

===== PAGE: select_datasets.html | Select Datasets =====
## Select Datasets
 After selecting your  study level exports  data sources , you land on the Datasets screen. Depending on the data types you selected, you can select individual datasets to export. 
 Note : There can be intermediate steps between the Select Data Source step and the Select Datasets step for some export types, such as Transformed Data. In those cases, follow the prompts until you reach the Select Datasets step. 
## Select Datasets to Extract
- From the Select Dataset step:
- Select the output format of each data type.
 Note : The comma-separated value (CSV) output format is the default selection. When extracting Rave data type, you can also choose to output data files in the XPT format, or in both CSV and XPT. For Rave data only, when you select both types, the extracted zip file contains duplicate sets of files in both CSV and XPT formats. 
- Select datasets to extract.
- Select the version of the dataset to extract.
 Important : Select  ALL for Veeva Connect datasets.
- Click Continue .
## Next Step
 Schedule Export

===== PAGE: set_data_export_type.html | Set Data Export Type =====
## Set Data Export Type
 After setting the run schedule for the export, you land on the Data Export Type screen. You can choose what data to export relative to the latest executed export. You can export all available data or to export only the data changes since the latest export execution. 
 Data export options differ by data type. For a table comparing the data types, see Export Configuration Compatibility by Data Type . 
## Select an Export Type
- On the Set Export Type screen, select a data export type:
- Cumulative – Each export instance exports all available data. 
- Incremental – Each export instance only exports data changes since the previous run. The first incremental run is cumulative.
 Note : Incremental data export is not available for Audit and EDC data. 
- Click Continue to go to the Outputs screen.

 Name Outputs

===== PAGE: sql-based_post_processing_for_exports.html | SQL-based Post Processing for Exports =====
## SQL-based Post Processing for Exports
## Description
 For select users, you can configure export jobs to perform post processing by entering SQL code.
## Affected Roles
 Select Internal users with the Extractor Admin building block on their platform role and other clearance.
## Advantages
 You can perform post processing on extracted files so that the downloaded files appear in a format that fits your specific requirements.  
## User Impact
 For users with the correct permissions, the Select Source step of the New Export Job workflow includes the Enable Post Processing option. Enabling the option adds the Post Processing step between the Select Data and Schedule Job Runs steps. The Post Processing step includes a form where you can configure post processing by entering SQL code and other settings.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None.
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: standardize_analyte_units_with_unit_conversions.html | Standardize Analyte Units with Unit Conversions =====
## Standardize Analyte Units with Unit Conversions
 You can standardize analyte units in Data Connect Transformations to prepare datasets for regulatory submissions. This process uses derived datasets to apply pre-defined unit conversions from a centralized reference dictionary.
## Prerequisites
 Category 
 Requirement 
 Learn More 
 | Building Blocks 
 | Each of the following: 
- Manage Transformation Projects 
- Manage Data Sources 
 | Data Connect Roles and Capabilities 
## Understand Reference Dictionaries
 Your organization can use one of two reference dictionary types to enforce global mandates. 
- Custom Dictionary – A dictionary that your provides using an Application Programming Interface (API). Your organization maintains complete control over this dictionary 
- Default Dictionary – An open-source dictionary called Quantities, Units, Dimensions and Data Types (QUDT). QUDT converts conventional or United States units to Standard International (SI) units. . 
## Standardize and Convert Units
## Create Derived Dataset Project
- Access Data Connect . 
- Go to Transformations , click Add Project , and enter the following details: 
- Project Name : Enter a unique project name. 
- Project Type: Select Derived datasets . 
- Setup From : Select From scratch . 
- Click Add project . 
## Configure Unit Conversion
- On the right side of the project row, click More > View / Modify to open the canvas. 
- Drag your source dataset (for example: LBCHEM) from the left-hand Data Source panel to the target canvas. 
- Select the dataset node, click the More icon, and select Unit conversion from the operator menu 
- On the Dataset Details step, enter the following details: 
- New dataset name – Enter a unique name for the new dataset. You cannot alter the original source node name. 
- Select current dataset layout – Select the structure of the source dataset. Select Horizontal if the dataset contains individual variables for units and analytes. 
- Selection conversion output – Select your target format (for example, SI unit). 
- Click Continue . 
- On the Source Units Definition step, map the unit columns to their corresponding analytes: 
- Source unit columns – Select the column containing unit values (for example, Sodium_Unit). 
- Select the value column (for example, Sodium) and map it to the corresponding unit column. 
- Click Continue . 
- On the Result Columns step, define the output column naming convention to differentiate between original and converted variables: 
- Enter a prefix or suffix for the Result value column (for example, _values). 
- Enter a prefix or suffix for the Result unit column (for example, _units). 
- Click Continue . 
- Click Apply Unit Conversion . 
## System Behavior and Data Processing
- Asynchronous Processing – The unit conversion process runs in the background and takes several minutes depending on the dataset size. You can navigate away from the canvas to work on other projects without interrupting the conversion. 
- Completed Status – Once the conversion finishes, a new node appears on the canvas with a link indicating unit conversion. 
- Unmapped Units – If the dictionary does not contain a unit or analyte, the system flags the record as Not available and outputs a null value. 
- Multiple Matches – If the system finds multiple units for a single analyte, it shows an alert. You must open the configuration and manually select the correct unit from the dictionary.

===== PAGE: store_extracts_on_secure_ftp_sites.html | Store Extracts on Secure FTP Sites =====
## Store Extracts on Secure FTP Sites
## Description
 With the new Extractor Admin permission, you can define a   client hosted  secure FTP (SFTP) storage location to use as the preferred storage location for data extractions. When creating a new export job, you can select a defined SFTP location as the preferred output destination. When the export job executes, the data extracts transfer automatically to the selected client hosted SFTP location. 
## Affected Roles
- Users with the new  Extractor Admin permission can define and edit SFTP locations using the Settings menu. 
- When creating a new export job, users with the Data Extract permission can select a preconfigured SFTP destination for extracted data sets. 
## Advantages
 Data sets stored on client defined SFTP servers are not subject to a 24-hour download limitation. You can retrieve those data  anytime after the export job has completed successfully.
## User Impact
- While creating export jobs.  you can select a preconfigured SFTP destination to ensure that data extracts from the export job are available beyond the 24-hour limit imposed by MEDS Extractor. 
- If you have the  Extractor Admin permissions, you see the new  Settings option on the MEDS Extractor job list page. You can also choose a predefined SFTP location for extracts, as part of selecting output destinations. 
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: study_master_data_extraction_now_available.html | Study Master Data Extraction Now Available =====
## Study Master Data Extraction Now Available
## Description
 You can configure export jobs to extract Study Level Master data from Cloud Administration studies. Study Master data includes team membership, study assignments, and study site addresses for the selected study environment. 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the Extract Master Data building block.
## Advantages
 You can extract  Study Level Master  data from your Cloud Administration studies.    
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, you can add the Extract Master Data building block to your customer-defined platform role.
 In MEDS Extractor:
- You can select the Master Data data type when creating export jobs. 
- CTMS operational data extracts no longer contain the "Study master" file. You can find that file in Study Master data extracts with the new name "Study Site Addresses". 
 Study Level Master data extracts reflect data entered into Cloud Administration such as client division information, study site information, and Principal Investigator information in three files: 
- Team Membership 
- Study Assignment 
- Study Site Addresses 
 When testing this feature, consider comparing the extracted data from this export job type to data entered into Cloud Administration. 
## Special Considerations
 When you select t he Master Data type, you cannot select either of the Audit Master Data or Audit CTMS data types.

===== PAGE: super_user_role_now_available.html | Super User Role Now Available =====
## Super User Role Now Available
## Description
 You can now apply the Super User role to MEDS Extractor users. Users with the Super User role can view all jobs within the client division across the studies the user has access to. Visible jobs include both jobs that user created, as well as jobs other MEDS Extractor users created.
## Affected Roles
 All platform roles with the View as Super User building block.
## Advantages
 With the Super User role, you can set specific users to have greater visibility of export jobs within a client division.
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, while creating or editing a platform role, you can select the Data Extraction > View as Super User building block.
 When a Super User enters MEDS Extractor, on the Export Jobs screen, that user can:
- See all export jobs across all studies they have access to. The Export Jobs screen includes the new Created By column that shows the name of the user who created the job.  
- Sort jobs by the Created By column. 
- Filter jobs by the Created By parameter. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Extractor Knowledge Space provides new and updated content to support this feature: 
- MEDS Extractor Roles and Permissions (updated) 
- Review Export Jobs List (updated) 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

## Support for Derived Data Sets
## What Is New?
 You can create derived data sets based on a combination various parameters. This feature introduces the following capabilities: 
- No-code data set operations, configurations, and edits including:
- Duplication 
- Grouping 
- Pivot and unpivot 
- Union 
- Subset 
- Joins 
- The ability to add derived variables to derived data sets 
- Field mapping from a single source data set or current target data set to the derived variable using manually entered values 
- No-code field transformation configuration to enable further derived variable customization 
- Generated dataset live preview  
- Data lineage of the transformation steps with indicators for root causes when errors occur  
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All users.

 You can create derived data sets for data projects created with the Create from Existing Data Sources option.

===== PAGE: system_error_for_active_studies_with_name_edits.html | System Error for Active Studies with Name Edits =====
## System Error for Active Studies with Name Edits
 Editing a study name in Cloud Administration after the study becomes active causes system errors in Data Connect . 
## Symptoms
 Downstream services in Data Surveillance and other Clinical Data Studio capabilities experience errors or fail to process data . 
## Causes
 You edited the study name after the study reached an active state . 
 Important : Medidata does not support editing study names after you create study artifacts in Data Connect, such as projects, templates, imports, exports, custom code, or datasets. 
## Diagnostic
 Review the study status to verify if it is active. Review the study history to verify if you edited the study name after creating study artifacts. 
## Resolution
- Please contact Medidata Support.
 Important : Do not edit study names after a study becomes active.

## User Impact
 If you have applied filters to a table, clicking the Reset Filters button clears those filters. When you refresh a page with active filters, the chosen filters remain applied to the table. Your use of Extractor is otherwise unchanged.

===== PAGE: transform_variable_mappings.html | Transform Variable Mappings =====
## Transform Variable Mappings
 When performing source-to-target mapping, you can use the Transformation Instructions tool to convert mapped variables to fit your needs.
- From the Create Transformations screen, create the transformation using one of the following methods:
 Important :
- You can only use a single method per transformation. You cannot use no-code modules and custom code in a single transformation. 
- You must use the standardized Date_INT field as the source field in transformations, rather than the Date field. For details, see  Interpolated Variables Behavior in Data Surveillance and RBQM 
- Transform with Modules – Use the Add Function menu while in the Field Transformation and Calculated Fields tabs to configure each transformation . You can also use the Add Condition button to add IF-THEN conditional logic to your transformation.
 Note :
- Some operations assume that you mapped multiple source variables to the target variable. 
- You can nest calculated fields to create complex calculations. 
- The Is Missing and Is Not Missing conditions are incompatible with data in the Time format. As a workaround:
- Create a calculated field for the Time field to convert the Time field to a String field. 
- In Field Transformation, apply Is Missing or Is Not Missing to the calculated field. 
- Transform with Custom Code – Enter the input transformation logic in the Custom Code tab's Code field with postgres SQL.
 Important : The Custom Code transformation option is deprecated. If you already transformed a dataset using custom code, the transformation still functions as configured. However, once you remove the custom code configuration, you can no longer access this option. 
 Tip : Custom Code Usage Notes 
 Use the form’s name as the form’s alias and project type as the target’s alias; for example: AE.field1 || CUST.AEOUT.
 Code validation includes the following error messages:
 Click here to expand... 
 Error 
 Description 
 Resolution 
 | Complete rule or function to save transformation 
 | There are incomplete fields in the transformation logic.  
 | Complete all empty fields. 
 | Calculated Field name… is already in use 
 | The Calculated Fields tab has a field with a name already in use. 
 | Update the field name. 
 | 
 Calculated field  cannot be deleted it is used because it  is used in other rules or Functions
 | You tried to delete a calculated field that other transformations or calculated fields use. 
 | Delete a different field as necessary. 
 | Transformation… has dependency on… So this operation is not allowed 
 | You tried to edit a field in such a way that causes an infinite loop. For example, when Field 2 uses Field 1, you update Field 1 so that Field 1 also uses Field 2. Therefore Field 1 and Field 2 endlessly refer to each other. 
 | Edit a different field as necessary. 
- Click the Refresh button in the bottom panel to preview the transformation. 
- Click the Quality Control tab to see any quality issues you must address. 
- Close the Edit Transformation dialog after resolving all quality issues.

===== PAGE: transformation_project_statuses.html | Transformation Project Statuses =====
## Transformation Project Statuses
 Transformations projects have the following statuses.
 Status 
 Description 
 | Not Started 
 | This is the default state of a new project when no user has applied a data source or configuration to it. 
 | In Progress 
 | 
 A user is still configuring the project. The project is unavailable for data consumption by other applications.
 Tip : Most datasets publish within 5-10 minutes, but can take longer depending on the complexity of the transformation. If the dataset gets stuck on the In-Progress step, try waiting 5 minutes and then republish the dataset. 
 | Publishing 
 | 
 A user  published the project , and the system is processing it.
 The system validates the dataset and applies the required infrastructure automatically. Users cannot view or modify the project while it is in this state.
 Tip : Most datasets publish within 5-10 minutes, but can take longer depending on the complexity of the transformation. There is also an approximately 10-minute time period between when the publication is complete and when the published records appear .  If a dataset takes longer than a total of 2 hours to publish and populate records  please contact Medidata Support. 
 | Published 
 | The system is now applying the project's transformations to new data records in your data stream.
 Users can still view and modify the project. If a user makes changes, the project reverts to the Publishing state. After republishing the changes, the project's version increments. The system only applies the latest project version, although users can view snapshots of previous versions.
 | Deactivating 
 | A user deactivated the project, and the system is processing it.
 The system takes a snapshot of the last available state, and continues to provide that snapshot to downstream applications that depend on the related datasets. This is to mitigate unintentional breaking impacts to downstream applications.
 | Inactive 
 | The project is no longer published.
 Users can deactivate a project as necessary, and can republish deactivated projects. When a user republishes a deactivated project, it reverts to the Publishing state. During the publishing process, the system reconciles the gap between the last snapshot and the latest data.
 | Error 
 | When errors occur, try republishing the project. If the error persists, please contact Medidata Support. 
 | Archiving 
 | A user archived the project, and the system is processing it.  
 | Archived 
 | The project is archived and no longer appears in the Transformations project list.
 If you archive a project with activated datasets , then any data already delivered downstream to Data Surveillance and RBQM remains there as stale data. Downstream users do not receive notifications if an activated dataset has been archived in Data Connect.

===== PAGE: transformations.html | Transformations =====
## Transformations
 Data Connect's Transformations capability provides the ability to manage heterogenous datasets to industry and company standards. It is a family of platform capabilities that integrate with Medidata's analytics solutions, reducing time and resources for Sponsors and CROs that perform activities including clinical data collection, clinical insights, and submission preparation. 
## What Is Transformations?
 Transformations is a family of platform functionalities that integrate with Medidata platform through Clinical Data Studio. Its capabilities include: 
- Generating SDTM-like datasets 
- Generating custom datasets across multiple source datasets that you can use to create derived datasets 
- Configuring and enriching custom data models that you can use to activate non-Medidata data sources and prepare them to be ready for other applications 
## What Is the Purpose of Transformations?
 Transformations enables non-technical users to standardize and transform any data sources into any data standard. Target data standards can be an industry standard, such as SDTM, or to a custom standard. 
## Who Uses Transformations?
 Data managers, clinical data scientists, biostatisticians, data scientists, and programmers.
## How Does Transformations Work?
 Transformations pulls data from various sources including: the internal data, such as Rave data; and external data that you can import such as Labs data. With these data, you can build data transformation projects and data models that serve different cases, including: 
- Custom Data Models 
- Datasets Enriched with Unit Conversions 
- Derived Datasets 
- SDTM-like datasets 
 Important :  Unsupported Data 
 Data Connect does not support variables such as coding dictionaries, coded terms, and code lists. While you can import datasets that includes these data, you cannot see or use them in Data Connect. However, these data can appear as-is in RBQM and Data Surveillance when you activate the associated dataset. For details, see Unsupported Variables in Data Connect . 
 These data projects include built-in validation tools so you can address issues as you customize them. Once validated, you can then publish the data projects so that the associated transformations apply to the affected data in other capabilities in Clinical Data Studio, such as  Data Surveillance . You can also download snapshots of the transformed data through export jobs .
 Important : Metadata updates from Rave sources can take up to 24 hours to update in Data Connect.
## What Are the Benefits of Transformations?
 Transformations serves various user groups and user types including Life Sciences Sponsors, Clinical Research Organizations (CROs), Medidata Internal Service Organization, and the following: 
- Data Managers and Clinical Data Scientists – Reduces programming skill requirements so these roles can focus on using their expertise in data standards and structure. 
- Biostatisticians and Data Scientists – Reduces time and effort on data engineering tasks, and increases bandwidth to focus on data analysis tasks. 
- Programmers – Reduces the workload during the database lock period, so that they can focus on solving edge cases and building pipelines for future automation. 
 Transformations bases its core user experience on three key principles: 
- Precision and Intention – Data transformation is a precise and intentional task. It is therefore important to do it right the first time, and pinpoint the root cause of a given error without leaving room for assumption or interpretation. 
- Reproducibility – Reduce the number of repetitive tasks as much as possible with 100% accuracy and transparency. 
- Interoperability and Scalability – Being data model agnostic allows Transformations to digest any data structure and output to any other data model with an infrastructure that supports the increasing scale of data velocity, data volume, and data variety. 
## How Do I Access Transformations?
 Access Data Connect , and click   Transformations   in the left-hand menu. Y ou can also click Add Datasets > Derived Datasets from the Datasets screen as a shortcut to create a derived dataset project . 
 Important : Supported Browsers 
- Chrome – Version 129 or later 
- Safari – 18.0 (MacOS Sequoia 15.0) or later

===== PAGE: transformations_-_ability_to_save_customized_datasets_as_templates.html | Transformations: Ability to Save Customized Datasets as Templates =====
## Transformations: Ability to Save Customized Datasets as Templates
## What Is New?
 When users publish certain transformation projects, they can save their target configurations as a template for reuse. Users can save configurations from custom SDTM-like datasets, derived datasets, and custom data models. Any user within the study group can use select these when creating custom SDTM projects.
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All. 
## Workflow Process
 When users attempt to publish one of the aforementioned data project types, a popup appears giving the user an option to save the project's configuration as a template. If the user saves the template, that template appears as an option when creating a custom SDTM dataset. Custom SDTM datasets created in this way begin with preselected domains based on the selected template. 
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications. 
## How Do I Benefit?
 The ability to save configurations enables different users such as programmers, data analysts, and data scientists to use different data models based on SDTM. This allows users to standardizes their study data for their specific use cases, such as analysis, visualization, or submission. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 When publishing one of the aforementioned data projects: 
- A popup appears giving the user an option to save the project's configuration as a template. 
 When creating an SDTM dataset:
- There is a new Target Setup option, where a user can select "Custom SDTM-like target". Once selected, the user can select one of the saved target SDTM templates. 
## Where Do I Learn More?
 The Data Connect documentation provides new and updated content to support this feature/enhancement: 
- Generate Custom Data Models 
- Generate Derived Datasets 
- Generate SDTM-like Datasets 
- Export Dataset Snapshots from Project 
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

## Transformations: Automated Mapping for SDTM Data Models and Unit Conversion
## What Is New?
 Users can now have Data Connect preselect certain data when building transformation projects. This occurs for the following project types: 
- SDTM-like Datasets – Data Connect can pre-maps variables for the selected domain. 
- Unit Conversions – Data Connect can provide SI unit suggestions based on the selected analyte. 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No impact to the workflow process. This update augments the existing data transformation project workflow and does not introduce new steps.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 The automated mapping speeds up project creation by inserting AI-suggested data based on common user scenarios.
## Where Do I See the Change?
 AI-enabled suggestions appear when users edit newly created SDTM-like data projects and unit conversion projects. For unit conversion projects, the suggestions specifically appear after selecting an analyte while configuring unit conversion groups.
 While editing custom SDTM-like datasets:
- The Run Auto-Mapping button appears on the dataset mapping screen. Clicking the button causes Data Connect to automatically map variables using using artificial intelligence (AI) capabilities. 
 While applying unit conversions for unit conversion projects: 
- After selecting an analyte, Data Connect automatically inserts the appropriate SI unit. 
- When manually selecting SI units, Data Connect provides suggestions based on users' selection history.

 Functionality or Module 
 | Transformations
 Summary of Changes 
 | 
 As highlighted in the Release Newsletter : 
 | Derived variables 
 | 
- From the Canvas after creating a dataset, you can now select  Add Derived Variable from the Operator list. 
- Helper fields: 
- Calculated Fields are now called Helper Fields. They are available on the same screen as the Derived Variable. This variable does not appear in the output dataset/node. You can use it with multiple derived variables. 
- Logic Condition: 
- Using multiple data types no longer requires Helper fields for data type conversion. Data Connect auto detects data types when you enter a Value.  
- The Concatenate option now has both Simple and Advanced options. 
- The Substring menu selection has By Position and By Delimiter options. 
- You can enter expressions while using the Math calculation. 
- Rank operations have new names: 
- RANK is now GROUP [First/Last].
- GROUP is now GROUP STATS [Min/Max/Avg/Sum]. 
- New Keys is now Grouping fields. 
- Select fields is now Select output fields. 
 | Field count 
 | 
- The Data Preview screen includes a field count beyond the count of records. 
 Benefit to Users 
 | Streamlines the creation of derived variables while adding more customization options.  
 Summary of User Impact 
 | 
 Impact to Users and Roles 
 All Transformation users.
 Impact to Workflow Processes 
 No impact to workflow processes.
